1→"""
     2→DM Pairing System
     3→
     4→Code-based approval flow for authorizing new users on messaging platforms.
     5→Instead of static allowlists with user IDs, unknown users receive a one-time
     6→pairing code that the bot owner approves via the CLI.
     7→
     8→Security features (based on OWASP + NIST SP 800-63-4 guidance):
     9→  - 8-char codes from 32-char unambiguous alphabet (no 0/O/1/I)
    10→  - Cryptographic randomness via secrets.choice()
    11→  - 1-hour code expiry
    12→  - Max 3 pending codes per platform
    13→  - Rate limiting: 1 request per user per 10 minutes
    14→  - Lockout after 5 failed approval attempts (1 hour)
    15→  - File permissions: chmod 0600 on all data files
    16→  - Codes are never logged to stdout
    17→
    18→Storage: ~/.hermes/pairing/
    19→"""
    20→
    21→import json
    22→import os
    23→import secrets
    24→import time
    25→from pathlib import Path
    26→from typing import Optional
    27→
    28→
    29→# Unambiguous alphabet -- excludes 0/O, 1/I to prevent confusion
    30→ALPHABET = "ABCDEFGHJKLMNPQRSTUVWXYZ23456789"
    31→CODE_LENGTH = 8
    32→
    33→# Timing constants
    34→CODE_TTL_SECONDS = 3600             # Codes expire after 1 hour
    35→RATE_LIMIT_SECONDS = 600            # 1 request per user per 10 minutes
    36→LOCKOUT_SECONDS = 3600              # Lockout duration after too many failures
    37→
    38→# Limits
    39→MAX_PENDING_PER_PLATFORM = 3        # Max pending codes per platform
    40→MAX_FAILED_ATTEMPTS = 5             # Failed approvals before lockout
    41→
    42→PAIRING_DIR = Path(os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/pairing"))
    43→
    44→
    45→def _secure_write(path: Path, data: str) -> None:
    46→    """Write data to file with restrictive permissions (owner read/write only)."""
    47→    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    48→    path.write_text(data, encoding="utf-8")
    49→    try:
    50→        os.chmod(path, 0o600)
    51→    except OSError:
    52→        pass  # Windows doesn't support chmod the same way
    53→
    54→
    55→class PairingStore:
    56→    """
    57→    Manages pairing codes and approved user lists.
    58→
    59→    Data files per platform:
    60→      - {platform}-pending.json   : pending pairing requests
    61→      - {platform}-approved.json  : approved (paired) users
    62→      - _rate_limits.json         : rate limit tracking
    63→    """
    64→
    65→    def __init__(self):
    66→        PAIRING_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    67→
    68→    def _pending_path(self, platform: str) -> Path:
    69→        return PAIRING_DIR / f"{platform}-pending.json"
    70→
    71→    def _approved_path(self, platform: str) -> Path:
    72→        return PAIRING_DIR / f"{platform}-approved.json"
    73→
    74→    def _rate_limit_path(self) -> Path:
    75→        return PAIRING_DIR / "_rate_limits.json"
    76→
    77→    def _load_json(self, path: Path) -> dict:
    78→        if path.exists():
    79→            try:
    80→                return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    81→            except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError):
    82→                return {}
    83→        return {}
    84→
    85→    def _save_json(self, path: Path, data: dict) -> None:
    86→        _secure_write(path, json.dumps(data, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))
    87→
    88→    # ----- Approved users -----
    89→
    90→    def is_approved(self, platform: str, user_id: str) -> bool:
    91→        """Check if a user is approved (paired) on a platform."""
    92→        approved = self._load_json(self._approved_path(platform))
    93→        return user_id in approved
    94→
    95→    def list_approved(self, platform: str = None) -> list:
    96→        """List approved users, optionally filtered by platform."""
    97→        results = []
    98→        platforms = [platform] if platform else self._all_platforms("approved")
    99→        for p in platforms:
   100→            approved = self._load_json(self._approved_path(p))
   101→            for uid, info in approved.items():
   102→                results.append({"platform": p, "user_id": uid, **info})
   103→        return results
   104→
   105→    def _approve_user(self, platform: str, user_id: str, user_name: str = "") -> None:
   106→        """Add a user to the approved list."""
   107→        approved = self._load_json(self._approved_path(platform))
   108→        approved[user_id] = {
   109→            "user_name": user_name,
   110→            "approved_at": time.time(),
   111→        }
   112→        self._save_json(self._approved_path(platform), approved)
   113→
   114→    def revoke(self, platform: str, user_id: str) -> bool:
   115→        """Remove a user from the approved list. Returns True if found."""
   116→        path = self._approved_path(platform)
   117→        approved = self._load_json(path)
   118→        if user_id in approved:
   119→            del approved[user_id]
   120→            self._save_json(path, approved)
   121→            return True
   122→        return False
   123→
   124→    # ----- Pending codes -----
   125→
   126→    def generate_code(
   127→        self, platform: str, user_id: str, user_name: str = ""
   128→    ) -> Optional[str]:
   129→        """
   130→        Generate a pairing code for a new user.
   131→
   132→        Returns the code string, or None if:
   133→          - User is rate-limited (too recent request)
   134→          - Max pending codes reached for this platform
   135→          - User/platform is in lockout due to failed attempts
   136→        """
   137→        self._cleanup_expired(platform)
   138→
   139→        # Check lockout
   140→        if self._is_locked_out(platform):
   141→            return None
   142→
   143→        # Check rate limit for this specific user
   144→        if self._is_rate_limited(platform, user_id):
   145→            return None
   146→
   147→        # Check max pending
   148→        pending = self._load_json(self._pending_path(platform))
   149→        if len(pending) >= MAX_PENDING_PER_PLATFORM:
   150→            return None
   151→
   152→        # Generate cryptographically random code
   153→        code = "".join(secrets.choice(ALPHABET) for _ in range(CODE_LENGTH))
   154→
   155→        # Store pending request
   156→        pending[code] = {
   157→            "user_id": user_id,
   158→            "user_name": user_name,
   159→            "created_at": time.time(),
   160→        }
   161→        self._save_json(self._pending_path(platform), pending)
   162→
   163→        # Record rate limit
   164→        self._record_rate_limit(platform, user_id)
   165→
   166→        return code
   167→
   168→    def approve_code(self, platform: str, code: str) -> Optional[dict]:
   169→        """
   170→        Approve a pairing code. Adds the user to the approved list.
   171→
   172→        Returns {user_id, user_name} on success, None if code is invalid/expired.
   173→        """
   174→        self._cleanup_expired(platform)
   175→        code = code.upper().strip()
   176→
   177→        pending = self._load_json(self._pending_path(platform))
   178→        if code not in pending:
   179→            self._record_failed_attempt(platform)
   180→            return None
   181→
   182→        entry = pending.pop(code)
   183→        self._save_json(self._pending_path(platform), pending)
   184→
   185→        # Add to approved list
   186→        self._approve_user(platform, entry["user_id"], entry.get("user_name", ""))
   187→
   188→        return {
   189→            "user_id": entry["user_id"],
   190→            "user_name": entry.get("user_name", ""),
   191→        }
   192→
   193→    def list_pending(self, platform: str = None) -> list:
   194→        """List pending pairing requests, optionally filtered by platform."""
   195→        results = []
   196→        platforms = [platform] if platform else self._all_platforms("pending")
   197→        for p in platforms:
   198→            self._cleanup_expired(p)
   199→            pending = self._load_json(self._pending_path(p))
   200→            for code, info in pending.items():
   201→                age_min = int((time.time() - info["created_at"]) / 60)
   202→                results.append({
   203→                    "platform": p,
   204→                    "code": code,
   205→                    "user_id": info["user_id"],
   206→                    "user_name": info.get("user_name", ""),
   207→                    "age_minutes": age_min,
   208→                })
   209→        return results
   210→
   211→    def clear_pending(self, platform: str = None) -> int:
   212→        """Clear all pending requests. Returns count removed."""
   213→        count = 0
   214→        platforms = [platform] if platform else self._all_platforms("pending")
   215→        for p in platforms:
   216→            pending = self._load_json(self._pending_path(p))
   217→            count += len(pending)
   218→            self._save_json(self._pending_path(p), {})
   219→        return count
   220→
   221→    # ----- Rate limiting and lockout -----
   222→
   223→    def _is_rate_limited(self, platform: str, user_id: str) -> bool:
   224→        """Check if a user has requested a code too recently."""
   225→        limits = self._load_json(self._rate_limit_path())
   226→        key = f"{platform}:{user_id}"
   227→        last_request = limits.get(key, 0)
   228→        return (time.time() - last_request) < RATE_LIMIT_SECONDS
   229→
   230→    def _record_rate_limit(self, platform: str, user_id: str) -> None:
   231→        """Record the time of a pairing request for rate limiting."""
   232→        limits = self._load_json(self._rate_limit_path())
   233→        key = f"{platform}:{user_id}"
   234→        limits[key] = time.time()
   235→        self._save_json(self._rate_limit_path(), limits)
   236→
   237→    def _is_locked_out(self, platform: str) -> bool:
   238→        """Check if a platform is in lockout due to failed approval attempts."""
   239→        limits = self._load_json(self._rate_limit_path())
   240→        lockout_key = f"_lockout:{platform}"
   241→        lockout_until = limits.get(lockout_key, 0)
   242→        return time.time() < lockout_until
   243→
   244→    def _record_failed_attempt(self, platform: str) -> None:
   245→        """Record a failed approval attempt. Triggers lockout after MAX_FAILED_ATTEMPTS."""
   246→        limits = self._load_json(self._rate_limit_path())
   247→        fail_key = f"_failures:{platform}"
   248→        fails = limits.get(fail_key, 0) + 1
   249→        limits[fail_key] = fails
   250→        if fails >= MAX_FAILED_ATTEMPTS:
   251→            lockout_key = f"_lockout:{platform}"
   252→            limits[lockout_key] = time.time() + LOCKOUT_SECONDS
   253→            limits[fail_key] = 0  # Reset counter
   254→            print(f"[pairing] Platform {platform} locked out for {LOCKOUT_SECONDS}s "
   255→                  f"after {MAX_FAILED_ATTEMPTS} failed attempts", flush=True)
   256→        self._save_json(self._rate_limit_path(), limits)
   257→
   258→    # ----- Cleanup -----
   259→
   260→    def _cleanup_expired(self, platform: str) -> None:
   261→        """Remove expired pending codes."""
   262→        path = self._pending_path(platform)
   263→        pending = self._load_json(path)
   264→        now = time.time()
   265→        expired = [
   266→            code for code, info in pending.items()
   267→            if (now - info["created_at"]) > CODE_TTL_SECONDS
   268→        ]
   269→        if expired:
   270→            for code in expired:
   271→                del pending[code]
   272→            self._save_json(path, pending)
   273→
   274→    def _all_platforms(self, suffix: str) -> list:
   275→        """List all platforms that have data files of a given suffix."""
   276→        platforms = []
   277→        for f in PAIRING_DIR.iterdir():
   278→            if f.name.endswith(f"-{suffix}.json"):
   279→                platform = f.name.replace(f"-{suffix}.json", "")
   280→                if not platform.startswith("_"):
   281→                    platforms.append(platform)
   282→        return platforms
   283→

1→"""
     2→CLI commands for the DM pairing system.
     3→
     4→Usage:
     5→    hermes pairing list              # Show all pending + approved users
     6→    hermes pairing approve <platform> <code>  # Approve a pairing code
     7→    hermes pairing revoke <platform> <user_id> # Revoke user access
     8→    hermes pairing clear-pending     # Clear all expired/pending codes
     9→"""
    10→
    11→def pairing_command(args):
    12→    """Handle hermes pairing subcommands."""
    13→    from gateway.pairing import PairingStore
    14→
    15→    store = PairingStore()
    16→    action = getattr(args, "pairing_action", None)
    17→
    18→    if action == "list":
    19→        _cmd_list(store)
    20→    elif action == "approve":
    21→        _cmd_approve(store, args.platform, args.code)
    22→    elif action == "revoke":
    23→        _cmd_revoke(store, args.platform, args.user_id)
    24→    elif action == "clear-pending":
    25→        _cmd_clear_pending(store)
    26→    else:
    27→        print("Usage: hermes pairing {list|approve|revoke|clear-pending}")
    28→        print("Run 'hermes pairing --help' for details.")
    29→
    30→
    31→def _cmd_list(store):
    32→    """List all pending and approved users."""
    33→    pending = store.list_pending()
    34→    approved = store.list_approved()
    35→
    36→    if not pending and not approved:
    37→        print("No pairing data found. No one has tried to pair yet~")
    38→        return
    39→
    40→    if pending:
    41→        print(f"\n  Pending Pairing Requests ({len(pending)}):")
    42→        print(f"  {'Platform':<12} {'Code':<10} {'User ID':<20} {'Name':<20} {'Age'}")
    43→        print(f"  {'--------':<12} {'----':<10} {'-------':<20} {'----':<20} {'---'}")
    44→        for p in pending:
    45→            print(
    46→                f"  {p['platform']:<12} {p['code']:<10} {p['user_id']:<20} "
    47→                f"{p.get('user_name', ''):<20} {p['age_minutes']}m ago"
    48→            )
    49→    else:
    50→        print("\n  No pending pairing requests.")
    51→
    52→    if approved:
    53→        print(f"\n  Approved Users ({len(approved)}):")
    54→        print(f"  {'Platform':<12} {'User ID':<20} {'Name':<20}")
    55→        print(f"  {'--------':<12} {'-------':<20} {'----':<20}")
    56→        for a in approved:
    57→            print(f"  {a['platform']:<12} {a['user_id']:<20} {a.get('user_name', ''):<20}")
    58→    else:
    59→        print("\n  No approved users.")
    60→
    61→    print()
    62→
    63→
    64→def _cmd_approve(store, platform: str, code: str):
    65→    """Approve a pairing code."""
    66→    platform = platform.lower().strip()
    67→    code = code.upper().strip()
    68→
    69→    result = store.approve_code(platform, code)
    70→    if result:
    71→        uid = result["user_id"]
    72→        name = result.get("user_name", "")
    73→        display = f"{name} ({uid})" if name else uid
    74→        print(f"\n  Approved! User {display} on {platform} can now use the bot~")
    75→        print(f"  They'll be recognized automatically on their next message.\n")
    76→    else:
    77→        print(f"\n  Code '{code}' not found or expired for platform '{platform}'.")
    78→        print(f"  Run 'hermes pairing list' to see pending codes.\n")
    79→
    80→
    81→def _cmd_revoke(store, platform: str, user_id: str):
    82→    """Revoke a user's access."""
    83→    platform = platform.lower().strip()
    84→
    85→    if store.revoke(platform, user_id):
    86→        print(f"\n  Revoked access for user {user_id} on {platform}.\n")
    87→    else:
    88→        print(f"\n  User {user_id} not found in approved list for {platform}.\n")
    89→
    90→
    91→def _cmd_clear_pending(store):
    92→    """Clear all pending pairing codes."""
    93→    count = store.clear_pending()
    94→    if count:
    95→        print(f"\n  Cleared {count} pending pairing request(s).\n")
    96→    else:
    97→        print("\n  No pending requests to clear.\n")
    98→

def _is_user_authorized(self, source: SessionSource) -> bool:
        """
        Check if a user is authorized to use the bot.
        
        Checks in order:
        1. Per-platform allow-all flag (e.g., DISCORD_ALLOW_ALL_USERS=true)
        2. Environment variable allowlists (TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USERS, etc.)
        3. DM pairing approved list
        4. Global allow-all (GATEWAY_ALLOW_ALL_USERS=true)
        5. Default: deny
        """
        # Home Assistant events are system-generated (state changes), not
        # user-initiated messages.  The HASS_TOKEN already authenticates the
        # connection, so HA events are always authorized.
        if source.platform == Platform.HOMEASSISTANT:
            return True

        user_id = source.user_id
        if not user_id:
            return False

        platform_env_map = {
            Platform.TELEGRAM: "TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USERS",
            Platform.DISCORD: "DISCORD_ALLOWED_USERS",
            Platform.WHATSAPP: "WHATSAPP_ALLOWED_USERS",
            Platform.SLACK: "SLACK_ALLOWED_USERS",
            Platform.SIGNAL: "SIGNAL_ALLOWED_USERS",
            Platform.EMAIL: "EMAIL_ALLOWED_USERS",
        }
        platform_allow_all_map = {
            Platform.TELEGRAM: "TELEGRAM_ALLOW_ALL_USERS",
            Platform.DISCORD: "DISCORD_ALLOW_ALL_USERS",
            Platform.WHATSAPP: "WHATSAPP_ALLOW_ALL_USERS",
            Platform.SLACK: "SLACK_ALLOW_ALL_USERS",
            Platform.SIGNAL: "SIGNAL_ALLOW_ALL_USERS",
            Platform.EMAIL: "EMAIL_ALLOW_ALL_USERS",
        }

        # Per-platform allow-all flag (e.g., DISCORD_ALLOW_ALL_USERS=true)
        platform_allow_all_var = platform_allow_all_map.get(source.platform, "")
        if platform_allow_all_var and os.getenv(platform_allow_all_var, "").lower() in ("true", "1", "yes"):
            return True

        # Check pairing store (always checked, regardless of allowlists)
        platform_name = source.platform.value if source.platform else ""
        if self.pairing_store.is_approved(platform_name, user_id):
            return True

        # Check platform-specific and global allowlists
        platform_allowlist = os.getenv(platform_env_map.get(source.platform, ""), "").strip()
        global_allowlist = os.getenv("GATEWAY_ALLOWED_USERS", "").strip()

   143→## Context Compression

   150→
   151→## Provider Routing (config.yaml only)
   152→
   153→These go in `~/.hermes/config.yaml` under the `provider_routing` section:
   154→
   155→| Key | Description |
   156→|-----|-------------|

   158→| `only` | List of provider slugs to allow (e.g., `["anthropic", "google"]`) |
   159→| `ignore` | List of provider slugs to skip |

   162→| `data_collection` | `"allow"` (default) or `"deny"` to exclude data-storing providers |

   164→:::tip

## Permissions Configuration in Hermes Agent - Comprehensive Report

I've thoroughly searched the hermes-agent codebase and found multiple layers of permissions configuration. Here's what I discovered:

### 1. **Dangerous Command Approval System**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/tools/approval.py`

This is the core dangerous command detection and approval system with:
- **DANGEROUS_PATTERNS**: 26+ patterns for destructive operations (rm -r, mkfs, DROP TABLE, chmod 777, etc.)
- **Approval Flow**: Users can choose: **once**, **session**, **always**, or **deny**
- **Permanent Allowlist**: Commands approved with "always" are saved to `~/.hermes/config.yaml` under `command_allowlist`
- **Environment Variables**: 
  - `HERMES_YOLO_MODE=1` - Bypass all approval prompts (dangerous command approval bypass)
  - `HERMES_EXEC_ASK=1` - Enable approval prompts in gateway mode
  - `HERMES_SESSION_KEY` - Session identifier for approval tracking

**CLI Flag:** `--yolo` - Bypass all dangerous command approval prompts

### 2. **User Authorization (Gateway/Messaging)**

**Files:** 
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/website/docs/user-guide/security.md`

**Authorization Check Order:**
1. Per-platform allow-all flags
2. DM pairing approved list
3. Platform-specific allowlists
4. Global allowlist
5. Global allow-all setting
6. Default: **deny**

**Environment Variables** (from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/website/docs/reference/environment-variables.md`):

| Variable | Purpose |
|----------|---------|
| `TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USERS` | Comma-separated user IDs |
| `DISCORD_ALLOWED_USERS` | Comma-separated user IDs |
| `WHATSAPP_ALLOWED_USERS` | Comma-separated phone numbers |
| `SLACK_ALLOWED_USERS` | Comma-separated Slack user IDs |
| `SIGNAL_ALLOWED_USERS` | Comma-separated E.164 phone numbers or UUIDs |
| `EMAIL_ALLOWED_USERS` | Comma-separated email addresses |
| `GATEWAY_ALLOWED_USERS` | Cross-platform global allowlist |
| `TELEGRAM_ALLOW_ALL_USERS` | Per-platform allow-all flag |
| `DISCORD_ALLOW_ALL_USERS` | Per-platform allow-all flag |
| `WHATSAPP_ALLOW_ALL_USERS` | Per-platform allow-all flag |
| `SLACK_ALLOW_ALL_USERS` | Per-platform allow-all flag |
| `SIGNAL_ALLOW_ALL_USERS` | Per-platform allow-all flag |
| `EMAIL_ALLOW_ALL_USERS` | Per-platform allow-all flag |
| `GATEWAY_ALLOW_ALL_USERS` | Global allow-all flag |
| `SIGNAL_GROUP_ALLOWED_USERS` | Group filtering for Signal |

### 3. **DM Pairing System**

**Files:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/pairing.py`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/pairing.py`

**Security Features:**
- 8-character codes from unambiguous 32-char alphabet (no 0/O/1/I)
- Cryptographic randomness via `secrets.choice()`
- 1-hour code TTL
- Max 3 pending codes per platform
- Rate limiting: 1 request per user per 10 minutes
- Lockout after 5 failed approval attempts (1 hour)
- File permissions: chmod 0600 on pairing data

**CLI Commands:**
```bash
hermes pairing list              # Show pending + approved users
hermes pairing approve <platform> <code>  # Approve a pairing code
hermes pairing revoke <platform> <user_id>  # Revoke user access
hermes pairing clear-pending     # Clear all expired/pending codes
```

**Storage:** `~/.hermes/pairing/` with files:
- `{platform}-pending.json` - pending codes
- `{platform}-approved.json` - approved users
- `_rate_limits.json` - rate limit tracking

### 4. **Config Files and Structure**

**Location:** `~/.hermes/` directory

**Files:**
- `config.yaml` - Contains `command_allowlist: []` (permanently allowed patterns)
- `.env` - API keys and secrets (chmod 0600)
- `auth.json` - OAuth credentials (chmod 0600)
- `pairing/` - DM pairing data (chmod 0600)

**Config Default** (from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/config.py`):
```yaml
command_allowlist: []  # Permanently allowed dangerous command patterns
quick_commands: {}     # Custom commands that bypass agent loop
```

### 5. **Terminal Backend Security**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/website/docs/user-guide/security.md`

Dangerous command checks are **skipped** for isolated backends:
- `docker` - Container isolation
- `singularity` - HPC containers
- `modal` - Cloud sandbox
- `daytona` - Cloud sandbox

These are checked for `local` and `ssh` backends.

### 6. **CLI Arguments**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py`

```bash
--yolo                    # Bypass all dangerous command approval prompts
```

This flag sets `HERMES_YOLO_MODE=1` environment variable.

### 7. **Container Security** (from security documentation)

Docker security flags applied to every container:
- `--cap-drop ALL` - Drop all Linux capabilities
- `--security-opt no-new-privileges` - Block privilege escalation
- `--pids-limit 256` - Process count limit
- `--tmpfs /tmp:rw,nosuid,size=512m` - Size-limited /tmp

### 8. **File Permission Hardening**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/tests/test_file_permissions.py`

Verified permissions:
- Config files: chmod 0600
- Pairing files: chmod 0600
- Home directory: chmod 0700
- Subdirectories: chmod 0700

### 9. **Documentation**

**Key Documentation Files:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/website/docs/user-guide/security.md` - Complete security model (5 layers)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/website/docs/user-guide/configuration.md` - Configuration reference
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/website/docs/reference/environment-variables.md` - All environment variables
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/README.md` - Quick start and migration instructions

This is a comprehensive, multi-layered permissions system covering dangerous command approval, user authorization on messaging platforms, DM pairing, container isolation, and file-level security hardening.

1→"""
     2→HermesAgentLoop -- Reusable Multi-Turn Agent Engine
     3→
     4→Runs the hermes-agent tool-calling loop using standard OpenAI-spec tool calling.
     5→Works with any server that returns ChatCompletion objects with tool_calls:
     6→    - Phase 1: OpenAI server type (VLLM, SGLang, OpenRouter, OpenAI API)
     7→    - Phase 2: ManagedServer with client-side tool call parser
     8→
     9→The loop passes tools= and checks response.choices[0].message.tool_calls,
    10→identical to hermes-agent's run_agent.py. Tool execution is dispatched via
    11→handle_function_call() from model_tools.py.
    12→"""
    13→
    14→import asyncio
    15→import concurrent.futures
    16→import json
    17→import logging
    18→import os
    19→import uuid
    20→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    21→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Set
    22→
    23→from model_tools import handle_function_call
    24→
    25→# Thread pool for running sync tool calls that internally use asyncio.run()
    26→# (e.g., mini-swe-agent's modal/docker/daytona backends). Running them in a separate
    27→# thread gives them a clean event loop so they don't deadlock inside Atropos's loop.
    28→# Size must be large enough for concurrent eval tasks (e.g., 89 TB2 tasks all
    29→# making tool calls). Too small = thread pool starvation, tasks queue for minutes.
    30→# Resized at runtime by HermesAgentBaseEnv.__init__ via resize_tool_pool().
    31→_tool_executor = concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=128)
    32→
    33→
    34→def resize_tool_pool(max_workers: int):
    35→    """
    36→    Replace the global tool executor with a new one of the given size.
    37→
    38→    Called by HermesAgentBaseEnv.__init__ based on config.tool_pool_size.
    39→    Safe to call before any tasks are submitted.
    40→    """
    41→    global _tool_executor
    42→    _tool_executor = concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_workers)
    43→    logger.info("Tool thread pool resized to %d workers", max_workers)
    44→
    45→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    46→
    47→
    48→@dataclass
    49→class ToolError:
    50→    """Record of a tool execution error during the agent loop."""
    51→
    52→    turn: int                  # Which turn the error occurred on
    53→    tool_name: str             # Which tool was called
    54→    arguments: str             # The arguments passed (truncated)
    55→    error: str                 # The error message
    56→    tool_result: str           # The raw result returned to the model
    57→
    58→
    59→@dataclass
    60→class AgentResult:
    61→    """Result of running the agent loop."""
    62→
    63→    # Full conversation history in OpenAI message format
    64→    messages: List[Dict[str, Any]]
    65→    # ManagedServer.get_state() if available (Phase 2), None otherwise
    66→    managed_state: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None
    67→    # How many LLM calls were made
    68→    turns_used: int = 0
    69→    # True if model stopped calling tools naturally (vs hitting max_turns)
    70→    finished_naturally: bool = False
    71→    # Extracted reasoning content per turn (from PR #297 helpers)
    72→    reasoning_per_turn: List[Optional[str]] = field(default_factory=list)
    73→    # Tool errors encountered during the loop
    74→    tool_errors: List[ToolError] = field(default_factory=list)
    75→
    76→
    77→def _extract_reasoning_from_message(message) -> Optional[str]:
    78→    """
    79→    Extract reasoning content from a ChatCompletion message.
    80→
    81→    Handles multiple provider formats:
    82→    1. message.reasoning_content field (some providers)
    83→    2. message.reasoning field (some providers)
    84→    3. message.reasoning_details[].text (OpenRouter style)
    85→
    86→    Note: <think> block extraction from content is NOT done here -- that's
    87→    handled by the response already in Phase 1 (server does it) or by
    88→    ManagedServer's patch in Phase 2.
    89→
    90→    Args:
    91→        message: The assistant message from ChatCompletion response
    92→
    93→    Returns:
    94→        Extracted reasoning text, or None if not found
    95→    """
    96→    # Check reasoning_content field (common across providers)
    97→    if hasattr(message, "reasoning_content") and message.reasoning_content:
    98→        return message.reasoning_content
    99→
   100→    # Check reasoning field
   101→    if hasattr(message, "reasoning") and message.reasoning:
   102→        return message.reasoning
   103→
   104→    # Check reasoning_details (OpenRouter style)
   105→    if hasattr(message, "reasoning_details") and message.reasoning_details:
   106→        for detail in message.reasoning_details:
   107→            if hasattr(detail, "text") and detail.text:
   108→                return detail.text
   109→            if isinstance(detail, dict) and detail.get("text"):
   110→                return detail["text"]
   111→
   112→    return None
   113→
   114→
   115→class HermesAgentLoop:
   116→    """
   117→    Runs hermes-agent's tool-calling loop using standard OpenAI-spec tool calling.
   118→
   119→    Same pattern as run_agent.py:
   120→    - Pass tools= to the API
   121→    - Check response.choices[0].message.tool_calls
   122→    - Dispatch via handle_function_call()
   123→
   124→    Works identically with any server type -- OpenAI, VLLM, SGLang, OpenRouter,
   125→    or ManagedServer with a parser. The server determines how tool_calls get
   126→    populated on the response.
   127→    """
   128→
   129→    def __init__(
   130→        self,
   131→        server,
   132→        tool_schemas: List[Dict[str, Any]],
   133→        valid_tool_names: Set[str],
   134→        max_turns: int = 30,
   135→        task_id: Optional[str] = None,
   136→        temperature: float = 1.0,
   137→        max_tokens: Optional[int] = None,
   138→        extra_body: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
   139→    ):
   140→        """
   141→        Initialize the agent loop.
   142→
   143→        Args:
   144→            server: Server object with chat_completion() method (OpenAIServer,
   145→                    ManagedServer, ServerManager, etc.)
   146→            tool_schemas: OpenAI-format tool definitions from get_tool_definitions()
   147→            valid_tool_names: Set of tool names the model is allowed to call
   148→            max_turns: Maximum number of LLM calls before stopping
   149→            task_id: Unique ID for terminal/browser session isolation
   150→            temperature: Sampling temperature for generation
   151→            max_tokens: Max tokens per generation (None for server default)
   152→            extra_body: Extra parameters passed to the OpenAI client's create() call.
   153→                        Used for OpenRouter provider preferences, transforms, etc.
   154→                        e.g. {"provider": {"ignore": ["DeepInfra"]}}
   155→        """
   156→        self.server = server
   157→        self.tool_schemas = tool_schemas
   158→        self.valid_tool_names = valid_tool_names
   159→        self.max_turns = max_turns
   160→        self.task_id = task_id or str(uuid.uuid4())
   161→        self.temperature = temperature
   162→        self.max_tokens = max_tokens
   163→        self.extra_body = extra_body
   164→
   165→    async def run(self, messages: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> AgentResult:
   166→        """
   167→        Execute the full agent loop using standard OpenAI tool calling.
   168→
   169→        Args:
   170→            messages: Initial conversation messages (system + user).
   171→                      Modified in-place as the conversation progresses.
   172→
   173→        Returns:
   174→            AgentResult with full conversation history, managed state, and metadata
   175→        """
   176→        reasoning_per_turn = []
   177→        tool_errors: List[ToolError] = []
   178→
   179→        # Per-loop TodoStore for the todo tool (ephemeral, dies with the loop)
   180→        from tools.todo_tool import TodoStore, todo_tool as _todo_tool
   181→        _todo_store = TodoStore()
   182→
   183→        # Extract user task from first user message for browser_snapshot context
   184→        _user_task = None
   185→        for msg in messages:
   186→            if msg.get("role") == "user":
   187→                content = msg.get("content", "")
   188→                if isinstance(content, str) and content.strip():
   189→                    _user_task = content.strip()[:500]  # Cap to avoid huge strings
   190→                break
   191→
   192→        import time as _time
   193→
   194→        for turn in range(self.max_turns):
   195→            turn_start = _time.monotonic()
   196→
   197→            # Build the chat_completion kwargs
   198→            chat_kwargs = {
   199→                "messages": messages,
   200→                "n": 1,
   201→                "temperature": self.temperature,
   202→            }
   203→
   204→            # Only pass tools if we have them
   205→            if self.tool_schemas:
   206→                chat_kwargs["tools"] = self.tool_schemas
   207→
   208→            # Only pass max_tokens if explicitly set
   209→            if self.max_tokens is not None:
   210→                chat_kwargs["max_tokens"] = self.max_tokens
   211→
   212→            # Inject extra_body for provider-specific params (e.g., OpenRouter
   213→            # provider preferences like banned/preferred providers, transforms)
   214→            if self.extra_body:
   215→                chat_kwargs["extra_body"] = self.extra_body
   216→
   217→            # Make the API call -- standard OpenAI spec
   218→            api_start = _time.monotonic()
   219→            try:
   220→                response = await self.server.chat_completion(**chat_kwargs)
   221→            except Exception as e:
   222→                api_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - api_start
   223→                logger.error("API call failed on turn %d (%.1fs): %s", turn + 1, api_elapsed, e)
   224→                return AgentResult(
   225→                    messages=messages,
   226→                    managed_state=self._get_managed_state(),
   227→                    turns_used=turn + 1,
   228→                    finished_naturally=False,
   229→                    reasoning_per_turn=reasoning_per_turn,
   230→                    tool_errors=tool_errors,
   231→                )
   232→
   233→            api_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - api_start
   234→
   235→            if not response or not response.choices:
   236→                logger.warning("Empty response on turn %d (api=%.1fs)", turn + 1, api_elapsed)
   237→                return AgentResult(
   238→                    messages=messages,
   239→                    managed_state=self._get_managed_state(),
   240→                    turns_used=turn + 1,
   241→                    finished_naturally=False,
   242→                    reasoning_per_turn=reasoning_per_turn,
   243→                    tool_errors=tool_errors,
   244→                )
   245→
   246→            assistant_msg = response.choices[0].message
   247→
   248→            # Extract reasoning content from the response (all provider formats)
   249→            reasoning = _extract_reasoning_from_message(assistant_msg)
   250→            reasoning_per_turn.append(reasoning)
   251→
   252→            # Check for tool calls -- standard OpenAI spec.
   253→            # Fallback: if response has no structured tool_calls but content
   254→            # contains raw tool call tags (e.g. <tool_call>), parse them using
   255→            # hermes-agent's standalone parsers. This handles the case where
   256→            # ManagedServer's ToolCallTranslator couldn't parse because vLLM
   257→            # isn't installed.
   258→            if (
   259→                not assistant_msg.tool_calls
   260→                and assistant_msg.content
   261→                and self.tool_schemas
   262→                and "<tool_call>" in (assistant_msg.content or "")
   263→            ):
   264→                try:
   265→                    from environments.tool_call_parsers import get_parser
   266→                    fallback_parser = get_parser("hermes")
   267→                    parsed_content, parsed_calls = fallback_parser.parse(
   268→                        assistant_msg.content
   269→                    )
   270→                    if parsed_calls:
   271→                        assistant_msg.tool_calls = parsed_calls
   272→                        if parsed_content is not None:
   273→                            assistant_msg.content = parsed_content
   274→                        logger.debug(
   275→                            "Fallback parser extracted %d tool calls from raw content",
   276→                            len(parsed_calls),
   277→                        )
   278→                except Exception:
   279→                    pass  # Fall through to no tool calls
   280→
   281→            if assistant_msg.tool_calls:
   282→                # Normalize tool calls to dicts — they may come as objects
   283→                # (OpenAI API) or dicts (vLLM ToolCallTranslator).
   284→                def _tc_to_dict(tc):
   285→                    if isinstance(tc, dict):
   286→                        return {
   287→                            "id": tc.get("id", f"call_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}"),
   288→                            "type": "function",
   289→                            "function": {
   290→                                "name": tc.get("function", {}).get("name", tc.get("name", "")),
   291→                                "arguments": tc.get("function", {}).get("arguments", tc.get("arguments", "{}")),
   292→                            },
   293→                        }
   294→                    return {
   295→                        "id": tc.id,
   296→                        "type": "function",
   297→                        "function": {
   298→                            "name": tc.function.name,
   299→                            "arguments": tc.function.arguments,
   300→                        },
   301→                    }
   302→
   303→                # Build the assistant message dict for conversation history
   304→                msg_dict: Dict[str, Any] = {
   305→                    "role": "assistant",
   306→                    "content": assistant_msg.content or "",
   307→                    "tool_calls": [_tc_to_dict(tc) for tc in assistant_msg.tool_calls],
   308→                }
   309→
   310→                # Preserve reasoning_content for multi-turn chat template handling
   311→                # (e.g., Kimi-K2's template renders <think> blocks differently
   312→                # for history vs. the latest turn based on this field)
   313→                if reasoning:
   314→                    msg_dict["reasoning_content"] = reasoning
   315→
   316→                messages.append(msg_dict)
   317→
   318→                # Execute each tool call via hermes-agent's dispatch
   319→                for tc in assistant_msg.tool_calls:
   320→                    # Handle both object (OpenAI) and dict (vLLM) formats
   321→                    if isinstance(tc, dict):
   322→                        tool_name = tc.get("function", {}).get("name", tc.get("name", ""))
   323→                        tool_args_raw = tc.get("function", {}).get("arguments", tc.get("arguments", "{}"))
   324→                    else:
   325→                        tool_name = tc.function.name
   326→                        tool_args_raw = tc.function.arguments
   327→
   328→                    # Validate tool name
   329→                    if tool_name not in self.valid_tool_names:
   330→                        tool_result = json.dumps(
   331→                            {
   332→                                "error": f"Unknown tool '{tool_name}'. "
   333→                                f"Available tools: {sorted(self.valid_tool_names)}"
   334→                            }
   335→                        )
   336→                        tool_errors.append(ToolError(
   337→                            turn=turn + 1, tool_name=tool_name,
   338→                            arguments=tool_args_raw[:200],
   339→                            error=f"Unknown tool '{tool_name}'",
   340→                            tool_result=tool_result,
   341→                        ))
   342→                        logger.warning(
   343→                            "Model called unknown tool '%s' on turn %d",
   344→                            tool_name, turn + 1,
   345→                        )
   346→                    else:
   347→                        # Parse arguments and dispatch
   348→                        try:
   349→                            args = json.loads(tool_args_raw)
   350→                        except json.JSONDecodeError:
   351→                            args = {}
   352→                            logger.warning(
   353→                                "Invalid JSON in tool call arguments for '%s': %s",
   354→                                tool_name, tool_args_raw[:200],
   355→                            )
   356→
   357→                        try:
   358→                            if tool_name == "terminal":
   359→                                backend = os.getenv("TERMINAL_ENV", "local")
   360→                                cmd_preview = args.get("command", "")[:80]
   361→                                logger.info(
   362→                                    "[%s] $ %s", self.task_id[:8], cmd_preview,
   363→                                )
   364→
   365→                            tool_submit_time = _time.monotonic()
   366→
   367→                            # Todo tool -- handle locally (needs per-loop TodoStore)
   368→                            if tool_name == "todo":
   369→                                tool_result = _todo_tool(
   370→                                    todos=args.get("todos"),
   371→                                    merge=args.get("merge", False),
   372→                                    store=_todo_store,
   373→                                )
   374→                                tool_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - tool_submit_time
   375→                            elif tool_name == "memory":
   376→                                tool_result = json.dumps({"error": "Memory is not available in RL environments."})
   377→                                tool_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - tool_submit_time
   378→                            elif tool_name == "session_search":
   379→                                tool_result = json.dumps({"error": "Session search is not available in RL environments."})
   380→                                tool_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - tool_submit_time
   381→                            else:
   382→                                # Run tool calls in a thread pool so backends that
   383→                                # use asyncio.run() internally (modal, docker, daytona) get
   384→                                # a clean event loop instead of deadlocking.
   385→                                loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   386→                                # Capture current tool_name/args for the lambda
   387→                                _tn, _ta, _tid = tool_name, args, self.task_id
   388→                                tool_result = await loop.run_in_executor(
   389→                                    _tool_executor,
   390→                                    lambda: handle_function_call(
   391→                                        _tn, _ta, task_id=_tid,
   392→                                        user_task=_user_task,
   393→                                    ),
   394→                                )
   395→                                tool_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - tool_submit_time
   396→
   397→                            # Log slow tools and thread pool stats for debugging
   398→                            pool_active = _tool_executor._work_queue.qsize()
   399→                            if tool_elapsed > 30:
   400→                                logger.warning(
   401→                                    "[%s] turn %d: %s took %.1fs (pool queue=%d)",
   402→                                    self.task_id[:8], turn + 1, tool_name,
   403→                                    tool_elapsed, pool_active,
   404→                                )
   405→                        except Exception as e:
   406→                            tool_result = json.dumps(
   407→                                {"error": f"Tool execution failed: {type(e).__name__}: {str(e)}"}
   408→                            )
   409→                            tool_errors.append(ToolError(
   410→                                turn=turn + 1, tool_name=tool_name,
   411→                                arguments=tool_args_raw[:200],
   412→                                error=f"{type(e).__name__}: {str(e)}",
   413→                                tool_result=tool_result,
   414→                            ))
   415→                            logger.error(
   416→                                "Tool '%s' execution failed on turn %d: %s",
   417→                                tool_name, turn + 1, e,
   418→                            )
   419→
   420→                        # Also check if the tool returned an error in its JSON result
   421→                        try:
   422→                            result_data = json.loads(tool_result)
   423→                            if isinstance(result_data, dict):
   424→                                err = result_data.get("error")
   425→                                exit_code = result_data.get("exit_code")
   426→                                if err and exit_code and exit_code < 0:
   427→                                    tool_errors.append(ToolError(
   428→                                        turn=turn + 1, tool_name=tool_name,
   429→                                        arguments=tool_args_raw[:200],
   430→                                        error=str(err),
   431→                                        tool_result=tool_result[:500],
   432→                                    ))
   433→                        except (json.JSONDecodeError, TypeError):
   434→                            pass
   435→
   436→                    # Add tool response to conversation
   437→                    tc_id = tc.get("id", "") if isinstance(tc, dict) else tc.id
   438→                    messages.append(
   439→                        {
   440→                            "role": "tool",
   441→                            "tool_call_id": tc_id,
   442→                            "content": tool_result,
   443→                        }
   444→                    )
   445→
   446→                turn_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - turn_start
   447→                logger.info(
   448→                    "[%s] turn %d: api=%.1fs, %d tools, turn_total=%.1fs",
   449→                    self.task_id[:8], turn + 1, api_elapsed,
   450→                    len(assistant_msg.tool_calls), turn_elapsed,
   451→                )
   452→
   453→            else:
   454→                # No tool calls -- model is done
   455→                msg_dict = {
   456→                    "role": "assistant",
   457→                    "content": assistant_msg.content or "",
   458→                }
   459→                if reasoning:
   460→                    msg_dict["reasoning_content"] = reasoning
   461→                messages.append(msg_dict)
   462→
   463→                turn_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - turn_start
   464→                logger.info(
   465→                    "[%s] turn %d: api=%.1fs, no tools (finished), turn_total=%.1fs",
   466→                    self.task_id[:8], turn + 1, api_elapsed, turn_elapsed,
   467→                )
   468→
   469→                return AgentResult(
   470→                    messages=messages,
   471→                    managed_state=self._get_managed_state(),
   472→                    turns_used=turn + 1,
   473→                    finished_naturally=True,
   474→                    reasoning_per_turn=reasoning_per_turn,
   475→                    tool_errors=tool_errors,
   476→                )
   477→
   478→        # Hit max turns without the model stopping
   479→        logger.info("Agent hit max_turns (%d) without finishing", self.max_turns)
   480→        return AgentResult(
   481→            messages=messages,
   482→            managed_state=self._get_managed_state(),
   483→            turns_used=self.max_turns,
   484→            finished_naturally=False,
   485→            reasoning_per_turn=reasoning_per_turn,
   486→            tool_errors=tool_errors,
   487→        )
   488→
   489→    def _get_managed_state(self) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
   490→        """
   491→        Get ManagedServer state if the server supports it.
   492→
   493→        Returns state dict with SequenceNodes containing tokens/logprobs/masks,
   494→        or None if the server doesn't support get_state() (e.g., regular OpenAI server).
   495→        """
   496→        if hasattr(self.server, "get_state"):
   497→            return self.server.get_state()
   498→        return None
   499→

1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""
     3→Hermes CLI - Main entry point.
     4→
     5→Usage:
     6→    hermes                     # Interactive chat (default)
     7→    hermes chat                # Interactive chat
     8→    hermes gateway             # Run gateway in foreground
     9→    hermes gateway start       # Start gateway as service
    10→    hermes gateway stop        # Stop gateway service
    11→    hermes gateway status      # Show gateway status
    12→    hermes gateway install     # Install gateway service
    13→    hermes gateway uninstall   # Uninstall gateway service
    14→    hermes setup               # Interactive setup wizard
    15→    hermes logout              # Clear stored authentication
    16→    hermes status              # Show status of all components
    17→    hermes cron                # Manage cron jobs
    18→    hermes cron list           # List cron jobs
    19→    hermes cron status         # Check if cron scheduler is running
    20→    hermes doctor              # Check configuration and dependencies
    21→    hermes honcho setup                    # Configure Honcho AI memory integration
    22→    hermes honcho status                   # Show Honcho config and connection status
    23→    hermes honcho sessions                 # List directory → session name mappings
    24→    hermes honcho map <name>               # Map current directory to a session name
    25→    hermes honcho peer                     # Show peer names and dialectic settings
    26→    hermes honcho peer --user NAME         # Set user peer name
    27→    hermes honcho peer --ai NAME           # Set AI peer name
    28→    hermes honcho peer --reasoning LEVEL   # Set dialectic reasoning level
    29→    hermes honcho mode                     # Show current memory mode
    30→    hermes honcho mode [hybrid|honcho|local]  # Set memory mode
    31→    hermes honcho tokens                   # Show token budget settings
    32→    hermes honcho tokens --context N       # Set session.context() token cap
    33→    hermes honcho tokens --dialectic N     # Set dialectic result char cap
    34→    hermes honcho identity                 # Show AI peer identity representation
    35→    hermes honcho identity <file>          # Seed AI peer identity from a file (SOUL.md etc.)
    36→    hermes honcho migrate                  # Step-by-step migration guide: OpenClaw native → Hermes + Honcho
    37→    hermes version             # Show version
    38→    hermes update              # Update to latest version
    39→    hermes uninstall           # Uninstall Hermes Agent
    40→    hermes sessions browse     # Interactive session picker with search
    41→    hermes claw migrate        # Migrate from OpenClaw to Hermes
    42→    hermes claw migrate --dry-run  # Preview migration without changes
    43→"""
    44→

    46→import os
    47→import sys
    48→from pathlib import Path

    61→        load_dotenv(dotenv_path=_user_env, encoding="utf-8")
    62→    except UnicodeDecodeError:
    63→        load_dotenv(dotenv_path=_user_env, encoding="latin-1")
    64→load_dotenv(dotenv_path=PROJECT_ROOT / '.env', override=False)
    65→
    66→# Point mini-swe-agent at ~/.hermes/ so it shares our config
    67→os.environ.setdefault("MSWEA_GLOBAL_CONFIG_DIR", str(get_hermes_home()))
    68→os.environ.setdefault("MSWEA_SILENT_STARTUP", "1")
    69→

    77→
    78→def _has_any_provider_configured() -> bool:
    79→    """Check if at least one inference provider is usable."""
    80→    from hermes_cli.config import get_env_path, get_hermes_home
    81→    from hermes_cli.auth import get_auth_status
    82→
    83→    # Check env vars (may be set by .env or shell).
    84→    # OPENAI_BASE_URL alone counts — local models (vLLM, llama.cpp, etc.)
    85→    # often don't require an API key.
    86→    from hermes_cli.auth import PROVIDER_REGISTRY
    87→
    88→    # Collect all provider env vars
    89→    provider_env_vars = {"OPENROUTER_API_KEY", "OPENAI_API_KEY", "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY", "ANTHROPIC_TOKEN", "OPENAI_BASE_URL"}
    90→    for pconfig in PROVIDER_REGISTRY.values():
    91→        if pconfig.auth_type == "api_key":
    92→            provider_env_vars.update(pconfig.api_key_env_vars)
    93→    if any(os.getenv(v) for v in provider_env_vars):
    94→        return True
    95→
    96→    # Check .env file for keys
    97→    env_file = get_env_path()
    98→    if env_file.exists():
    99→        try:
   100→            for line in env_file.read_text().splitlines():
   101→                line = line.strip()
   102→                if line.startswith("#") or "=" not in line:
   103→                    continue
   104→                key, _, val = line.partition("=")
   105→                val = val.strip().strip("'\"")
   106→                if key.strip() in provider_env_vars and val:
   107→                    return True
   108→        except Exception:
   109→            pass
   110→
   111→    # Check for Nous Portal OAuth credentials
   112→    auth_file = get_hermes_home() / "auth.json"
   113→    if auth_file.exists():
   114→        try:
   115→            import json
   116→            auth = json.loads(auth_file.read_text())
   117→            active = auth.get("active_provider")
   118→            if active:
   119→                status = get_auth_status(active)
   120→                if status.get("logged_in"):
   121→                    return True
   122→        except Exception:
   123→            pass
   124→
   125→    return False
   126→
   127→
   128→def _session_browse_picker(sessions: list) -> Optional[str]:
   129→    """Interactive curses-based session browser with live search filtering.
   130→
   131→    Returns the selected session ID, or None if cancelled.
   132→    Uses curses (not simple_term_menu) to avoid the ghost-duplication rendering
   133→    bug in tmux/iTerm when arrow keys are used.
   134→    """
   135→    if not sessions:
   136→        print("No sessions found.")
   137→        return None
   138→
   139→    # Try curses-based picker first
   140→    try:
   141→        import curses
   142→        import time as _time
   143→        from datetime import datetime
   144→
   145→        result_holder = [None]
   146→
   147→        def _relative_time(ts):
   148→            if not ts:
   149→                return "?"
   150→            delta = _time.time() - ts
   151→            if delta < 60:
   152→                return "just now"
   153→            elif delta < 3600:
   154→                return f"{int(delta / 60)}m ago"
   155→            elif delta < 86400:
   156→                return f"{int(delta / 3600)}h ago"
   157→            elif delta < 172800:
   158→                return "yesterday"
   159→            elif delta < 604800:
   160→                return f"{int(delta / 86400)}d ago"
   161→            else:
   162→                return datetime.fromtimestamp(ts).strftime("%Y-%m-%d")
   163→
   164→        def _format_row(s, max_x):
   165→            """Format a session row for display."""
   166→            title = (s.get("title") or "").strip()
   167→            preview = (s.get("preview") or "").strip()
   168→            source = s.get("source", "")[:6]
   169→            last_active = _relative_time(s.get("last_active"))
   170→            sid = s["id"][:18]
   171→
   172→            # Adaptive column widths based on terminal width
   173→            # Layout: [arrow 3] [title/preview flexible] [active 12] [src 6] [id 18]
   174→            fixed_cols = 3 + 12 + 6 + 18 + 6  # arrow + active + src + id + padding
   175→            name_width = max(20, max_x - fixed_cols)
   176→
   177→            if title:
   178→                name = title[:name_width]
   179→            elif preview:
   180→                name = preview[:name_width]
   181→            else:
   182→                name = sid
   183→
   184→            return f"{name:<{name_width}}  {last_active:<10}  {source:<5} {sid}"
   185→
   186→        def _match(s, query):
   187→            """Check if a session matches the search query (case-insensitive)."""
   188→            q = query.lower()
   189→            return (
   190→                q in (s.get("title") or "").lower()
   191→                or q in (s.get("preview") or "").lower()
   192→                or q in s.get("id", "").lower()
   193→                or q in (s.get("source") or "").lower()
   194→            )
   195→
   196→        def _curses_browse(stdscr):
   197→            curses.curs_set(0)
   198→            if curses.has_colors():
   199→                curses.start_color()
   200→                curses.use_default_colors()

1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""
     3→Model Tools Module
     4→
     5→Thin orchestration layer over the tool registry. Each tool file in tools/
     6→self-registers its schema, handler, and metadata via tools.registry.register().
     7→This module triggers discovery (by importing all tool modules), then provides
     8→the public API that run_agent.py, cli.py, batch_runner.py, and the RL
     9→environments consume.
    10→
    11→Public API (signatures preserved from the original 2,400-line version):
    12→    get_tool_definitions(enabled_toolsets, disabled_toolsets, quiet_mode) -> list
    13→    handle_function_call(function_name, function_args, task_id, user_task) -> str
    14→    TOOL_TO_TOOLSET_MAP: dict          (for batch_runner.py)
    15→    TOOLSET_REQUIREMENTS: dict         (for cli.py, doctor.py)
    16→    get_all_tool_names() -> list
    17→    get_toolset_for_tool(name) -> str
    18→    get_available_toolsets() -> dict
    19→    check_toolset_requirements() -> dict
    20→    check_tool_availability(quiet) -> tuple
    21→"""
    22→
    23→import json
    24→import asyncio
    25→import os
    26→import logging
    27→from typing import Dict, Any, List, Optional, Tuple
    28→
    29→from tools.registry import registry
    30→from toolsets import resolve_toolset, validate_toolset
    31→
    32→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    33→
    34→
    35→# =============================================================================
    36→# Async Bridging  (single source of truth -- used by registry.dispatch too)
    37→# =============================================================================
    38→
    39→def _run_async(coro):
    40→    """Run an async coroutine from a sync context.
    41→
    42→    If the current thread already has a running event loop (e.g., inside
    43→    the gateway's async stack or Atropos's event loop), we spin up a
    44→    disposable thread so asyncio.run() can create its own loop without
    45→    conflicting.
    46→
    47→    This is the single source of truth for sync->async bridging in tool
    48→    handlers. The RL paths (agent_loop.py, tool_context.py) also provide
    49→    outer thread-pool wrapping as defense-in-depth, but each handler is
    50→    self-protecting via this function.
    51→    """
    52→    try:
    53→        loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
    54→    except RuntimeError:
    55→        loop = None
    56→
    57→    if loop and loop.is_running():
    58→        import concurrent.futures
    59→        with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1) as pool:
    60→            future = pool.submit(asyncio.run, coro)
    61→            return future.result(timeout=300)
    62→    return asyncio.run(coro)
    63→
    64→
    65→# =============================================================================
    66→# Tool Discovery  (importing each module triggers its registry.register calls)
    67→# =============================================================================
    68→
    69→def _discover_tools():
    70→    """Import all tool modules to trigger their registry.register() calls.
    71→
    72→    Wrapped in a function so import errors in optional tools (e.g., fal_client
    73→    not installed) don't prevent the rest from loading.
    74→    """
    75→    _modules = [
    76→        "tools.web_tools",
    77→        "tools.terminal_tool",
    78→        "tools.file_tools",
    79→        "tools.vision_tools",
    80→        "tools.mixture_of_agents_tool",
    81→        "tools.image_generation_tool",
    82→        "tools.skills_tool",
    83→        "tools.skill_manager_tool",
    84→        "tools.browser_tool",
    85→        "tools.cronjob_tools",
    86→        "tools.rl_training_tool",
    87→        "tools.tts_tool",
    88→        "tools.todo_tool",
    89→        "tools.memory_tool",
    90→        "tools.session_search_tool",
    91→        "tools.clarify_tool",
    92→        "tools.code_execution_tool",
    93→        "tools.delegate_tool",
    94→        "tools.process_registry",
    95→        "tools.send_message_tool",
    96→        "tools.honcho_tools",
    97→        "tools.homeassistant_tool",
    98→        "tools.switch_model_tool",
    99→    ]
   100→    import importlib
   101→    for mod_name in _modules:
   102→        try:
   103→            importlib.import_module(mod_name)
   104→        except Exception as e:
   105→            logger.debug("Could not import %s: %s", mod_name, e)
   106→
   107→
   108→_discover_tools()
   109→
   110→# MCP tool discovery (external MCP servers from config)
   111→try:
   112→    from tools.mcp_tool import discover_mcp_tools
   113→    discover_mcp_tools()
   114→except Exception as e:
   115→    logger.debug("MCP tool discovery failed: %s", e)
   116→
   117→
   118→# =============================================================================
   119→# Backward-compat constants  (built once after discovery)
   120→# =============================================================================
   121→
   122→TOOL_TO_TOOLSET_MAP: Dict[str, str] = registry.get_tool_to_toolset_map()
   123→
   124→TOOLSET_REQUIREMENTS: Dict[str, dict] = registry.get_toolset_requirements()
   125→
   126→# Resolved tool names from the last get_tool_definitions() call.
   127→# Used by code_execution_tool to know which tools are available in this session.
   128→_last_resolved_tool_names: List[str] = []
   129→
   130→
   131→# =============================================================================
   132→# Legacy toolset name mapping  (old _tools-suffixed names -> tool name lists)
   133→# =============================================================================
   134→
   135→_LEGACY_TOOLSET_MAP = {
   136→    "web_tools": ["web_search", "web_extract"],
   137→    "terminal_tools": ["terminal"],
   138→    "vision_tools": ["vision_analyze"],
   139→    "moa_tools": ["mixture_of_agents"],
   140→    "image_tools": ["image_generate"],
   141→    "skills_tools": ["skills_list", "skill_view", "skill_manage"],
   142→    "browser_tools": [
   143→        "browser_navigate", "browser_snapshot", "browser_click",
   144→        "browser_type", "browser_scroll", "browser_back",
   145→        "browser_press", "browser_close", "browser_get_images",
   146→        "browser_vision"
   147→    ],
   148→    "cronjob_tools": ["schedule_cronjob", "list_cronjobs", "remove_cronjob"],
   149→    "rl_tools": [
   150→        "rl_list_environments", "rl_select_environment",
   151→        "rl_get_current_config", "rl_edit_config",
   152→        "rl_start_training", "rl_check_status",
   153→        "rl_stop_training", "rl_get_results",
   154→        "rl_list_runs", "rl_test_inference"
   155→    ],
   156→    "file_tools": ["read_file", "write_file", "patch", "search_files"],
   157→    "tts_tools": ["text_to_speech"],
   158→}
   159→
   160→
   161→# =============================================================================
   162→# get_tool_definitions  (the main schema provider)
   163→# =============================================================================
   164→
   165→def get_tool_definitions(
   166→    enabled_toolsets: List[str] = None,
   167→    disabled_toolsets: List[str] = None,
   168→    quiet_mode: bool = False,
   169→) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
   170→    """
   171→    Get tool definitions for model API calls with toolset-based filtering.
   172→
   173→    All tools must be part of a toolset to be accessible.
   174→
   175→    Args:
   176→        enabled_toolsets: Only include tools from these toolsets.
   177→        disabled_toolsets: Exclude tools from these toolsets (if enabled_toolsets is None).
   178→        quiet_mode: Suppress status prints.
   179→
   180→    Returns:
   181→        Filtered list of OpenAI-format tool definitions.
   182→    """
   183→    # Determine which tool names the caller wants
   184→    tools_to_include: set = set()
   185→
   186→    if enabled_toolsets:
   187→        for toolset_name in enabled_toolsets:
   188→            if validate_toolset(toolset_name):
   189→                resolved = resolve_toolset(toolset_name)
   190→                tools_to_include.update(resolved)
   191→                if not quiet_mode:
   192→                    print(f"✅ Enabled toolset '{toolset_name}': {', '.join(resolved) if resolved else 'no tools'}")
   193→            elif toolset_name in _LEGACY_TOOLSET_MAP:
   194→                legacy_tools = _LEGACY_TOOLSET_MAP[toolset_name]
   195→                tools_to_include.update(legacy_tools)
   196→                if not quiet_mode:
   197→                    print(f"✅ Enabled legacy toolset '{toolset_name}': {', '.join(legacy_tools)}")
   198→            else:
   199→                if not quiet_mode:
   200→                    print(f"⚠️  Unknown toolset: {toolset_name}")
   201→
   202→    elif disabled_toolsets:
   203→        from toolsets import get_all_toolsets
   204→        for ts_name in get_all_toolsets():
   205→            tools_to_include.update(resolve_toolset(ts_name))
   206→
   207→        for toolset_name in disabled_toolsets:
   208→            if validate_toolset(toolset_name):
   209→                resolved = resolve_toolset(toolset_name)
   210→                tools_to_include.difference_update(resolved)
   211→                if not quiet_mode:
   212→                    print(f"🚫 Disabled toolset '{toolset_name}': {', '.join(resolved) if resolved else 'no tools'}")
   213→            elif toolset_name in _LEGACY_TOOLSET_MAP:
   214→                legacy_tools = _LEGACY_TOOLSET_MAP[toolset_name]
   215→                tools_to_include.difference_update(legacy_tools)
   216→                if not quiet_mode:
   217→                    print(f"🚫 Disabled legacy toolset '{toolset_name}': {', '.join(legacy_tools)}")
   218→            else:
   219→                if not quiet_mode:
   220→                    print(f"⚠️  Unknown toolset: {toolset_name}")
   221→    else:
   222→        from toolsets import get_all_toolsets
   223→        for ts_name in get_all_toolsets():
   224→            tools_to_include.update(resolve_toolset(ts_name))
   225→
   226→    # Ask the registry for schemas (only returns tools whose check_fn passes)
   227→    filtered_tools = registry.get_definitions(tools_to_include, quiet=quiet_mode)
   228→
   229→    # Rebuild execute_code schema to only list sandbox tools that are actually
   230→    # enabled.  Without this, the model sees "web_search is available in
   231→    # execute_code" even when the user disabled the web toolset (#560-discord).
   232→    if "execute_code" in tools_to_include:
   233→        from tools.code_execution_tool import SANDBOX_ALLOWED_TOOLS, build_execute_code_schema
   234→        sandbox_enabled = SANDBOX_ALLOWED_TOOLS & tools_to_include
   235→        dynamic_schema = build_execute_code_schema(sandbox_enabled)
   236→        for i, td in enumerate(filtered_tools):
   237→            if td.get("function", {}).get("name") == "execute_code":
   238→                filtered_tools[i] = {"type": "function", "function": dynamic_schema}
   239→                break
   240→
   241→    if not quiet_mode:
   242→        if filtered_tools:
   243→            tool_names = [t["function"]["name"] for t in filtered_tools]
   244→            print(f"🛠️  Final tool selection ({len(filtered_tools)} tools): {', '.join(tool_names)}")
   245→        else:
   246→            print("🛠️  No tools selected (all filtered out or unavailable)")
   247→
   248→    global _last_resolved_tool_names
   249→    _last_resolved_tool_names = [t["function"]["name"] for t in filtered_tools]
   250→
   251→    return filtered_tools
   252→
   253→
   254→# =============================================================================
   255→# handle_function_call  (the main dispatcher)
   256→# =============================================================================
   257→
   258→# Tools whose execution is intercepted by the agent loop (run_agent.py)
   259→# because they need agent-level state (TodoStore, MemoryStore, etc.).
   260→# The registry still holds their schemas; dispatch just returns a stub error
   261→# so if something slips through, the LLM sees a sensible message.
   262→_AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS = {"todo", "memory", "session_search", "delegate_task", "switch_model"}
   263→
   264→
   265→def handle_function_call(
   266→    function_name: str,
   267→    function_args: Dict[str, Any],
   268→    task_id: Optional[str] = None,
   269→    user_task: Optional[str] = None,
   270→    enabled_tools: Optional[List[str]] = None,
   271→) -> str:
   272→    """
   273→    Main function call dispatcher that routes calls to the tool registry.
   274→
   275→    Args:
   276→        function_name: Name of the function to call.
   277→        function_args: Arguments for the function.
   278→        task_id: Unique identifier for terminal/browser session isolation.
   279→        user_task: The user's original task (for browser_snapshot context).
   280→        enabled_tools: Tool names enabled for this session.  When provided,
   281→                       execute_code uses this list to determine which sandbox
   282→                       tools to generate.  Falls back to the process-global
   283→                       ``_last_resolved_tool_names`` for backward compat.
   284→
   285→    Returns:
   286→        Function result as a JSON string.
   287→    """
   288→    # Notify the read-loop tracker when a non-read/search tool runs,
   289→    # so the *consecutive* counter resets (reads after other work are fine).
   290→    _READ_SEARCH_TOOLS = {"read_file", "search_files"}
   291→    if function_name not in _READ_SEARCH_TOOLS:
   292→        try:
   293→            from tools.file_tools import notify_other_tool_call
   294→            notify_other_tool_call(task_id or "default")
   295→        except Exception:
   296→            pass  # file_tools may not be loaded yet
   297→
   298→    try:
   299→        if function_name in _AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS:
   300→            return json.dumps({"error": f"{function_name} must be handled by the agent loop"})
   301→
   302→        if function_name == "execute_code":
   303→            # Prefer the caller-provided list so subagents can't overwrite
   304→            # the parent's tool set via the process-global.
   305→            sandbox_enabled = enabled_tools if enabled_tools is not None else _last_resolved_tool_names
   306→            return registry.dispatch(
   307→                function_name, function_args,
   308→                task_id=task_id,
   309→                enabled_tools=sandbox_enabled,
   310→            )
   311→
   312→        return registry.dispatch(
   313→            function_name, function_args,
   314→            task_id=task_id,
   315→            user_task=user_task,
   316→        )
   317→
   318→    except Exception as e:
   319→        error_msg = f"Error executing {function_name}: {str(e)}"
   320→        logger.error(error_msg)
   321→        return json.dumps({"error": error_msg}, ensure_ascii=False)
   322→
   323→
   324→# =============================================================================
   325→# Backward-compat wrapper functions
   326→# =============================================================================
   327→
   328→def get_all_tool_names() -> List[str]:
   329→    """Return all registered tool names."""
   330→    return registry.get_all_tool_names()
   331→
   332→
   333→def get_toolset_for_tool(tool_name: str) -> Optional[str]:
   334→    """Return the toolset a tool belongs to."""
   335→    return registry.get_toolset_for_tool(tool_name)
   336→
   337→
   338→def get_available_toolsets() -> Dict[str, dict]:
   339→    """Return toolset availability info for UI display."""
   340→    return registry.get_available_toolsets()
   341→
   342→
   343→def check_toolset_requirements() -> Dict[str, bool]:
   344→    """Return {toolset: available_bool} for every registered toolset."""
   345→    return registry.check_toolset_requirements()
   346→
   347→
   348→def check_tool_availability(quiet: bool = False) -> Tuple[List[str], List[dict]]:
   349→    """Return (available_toolsets, unavailable_info)."""
   350→    return registry.check_tool_availability(quiet=quiet)
   351→

   129→def list_available_providers() -> list[dict[str, str]]:
   130→    """Return info about all providers the user could use with ``provider:model``.
   131→
   132→    Each dict has ``id``, ``label``, and ``aliases``.
   133→    Checks which providers have valid credentials configured.
   134→    """
   135→    # Canonical providers in display order
   136→    _PROVIDER_ORDER = [
   137→        "openrouter", "nous", "openai-codex",
   138→        "zai", "kimi-coding", "minimax", "minimax-cn", "anthropic",
   139→    ]
   140→    # Build reverse alias map
   141→    aliases_for: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   142→    for alias, canonical in _PROVIDER_ALIASES.items():
   143→        aliases_for.setdefault(canonical, []).append(alias)
   144→
   145→    result = []
   146→    for pid in _PROVIDER_ORDER:
   147→        label = _PROVIDER_LABELS.get(pid, pid)
   148→        alias_list = aliases_for.get(pid, [])
   149→        # Check if this provider has credentials available
   150→        has_creds = False
   151→        try:
   152→            from hermes_cli.runtime_provider import resolve_runtime_provider
   153→            runtime = resolve_runtime_provider(requested=pid)
   154→            has_creds = bool(runtime.get("api_key"))
   155→        except Exception:
   156→            pass
   157→        result.append({
   158→            "id": pid,
   159→            "label": label,
   160→            "aliases": alias_list,
   161→            "authenticated": has_creds,
   162→        })
   163→    return result
