1→"""
     2→Process Registry -- In-memory registry for managed background processes.
     3→
     4→Tracks processes spawned via terminal(background=true), providing:
     5→  - Output buffering (rolling 200KB window)
     6→  - Status polling and log retrieval
     7→  - Blocking wait with interrupt support
     8→  - Process killing
     9→  - Crash recovery via JSON checkpoint file
    10→  - Session-scoped tracking for gateway reset protection
    11→
    12→Background processes execute THROUGH the environment interface -- nothing
    13→runs on the host machine unless TERMINAL_ENV=local. For Docker, Singularity,
    14→Modal, Daytona, and SSH backends, the command runs inside the sandbox.
    15→
    16→Usage:
    17→    from tools.process_registry import process_registry
    18→
    19→    # Spawn a background process (called from terminal_tool)
    20→    session = process_registry.spawn(env, "pytest -v", task_id="task_123")
    21→
    22→    # Poll for status
    23→    result = process_registry.poll(session.id)
    24→
    25→    # Block until done
    26→    result = process_registry.wait(session.id, timeout=300)
    27→
    28→    # Kill it
    29→    process_registry.kill(session.id)
    30→"""
    31→
    32→import json
    33→import logging
    34→import os
    35→import platform
    36→import shlex
    37→import shutil
    38→import signal
    39→import subprocess
    40→import threading
    41→import time
    42→import uuid
    43→
    44→_IS_WINDOWS = platform.system() == "Windows"
    45→from tools.environments.local import _find_shell, _HERMES_PROVIDER_ENV_BLOCKLIST
    46→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    47→from pathlib import Path
    48→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional
    49→
    50→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    51→
    52→
    53→# Checkpoint file for crash recovery (gateway only)
    54→CHECKPOINT_PATH = Path(os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/processes.json"))
    55→
    56→# Limits
    57→MAX_OUTPUT_CHARS = 200_000      # 200KB rolling output buffer
    58→FINISHED_TTL_SECONDS = 1800     # Keep finished processes for 30 minutes
    59→MAX_PROCESSES = 64              # Max concurrent tracked processes (LRU pruning)
    60→
    61→
    62→@dataclass
    63→class ProcessSession:
    64→    """A tracked background process with output buffering."""
    65→    id: str                                     # Unique session ID ("proc_xxxxxxxxxxxx")
    66→    command: str                                 # Original command string
    67→    task_id: str = ""                           # Task/sandbox isolation key
    68→    session_key: str = ""                       # Gateway session key (for reset protection)
    69→    pid: Optional[int] = None                   # OS process ID
    70→    process: Optional[subprocess.Popen] = None  # Popen handle (local only)
    71→    env_ref: Any = None                         # Reference to the environment object
    72→    cwd: Optional[str] = None                   # Working directory
    73→    started_at: float = 0.0                     # time.time() of spawn
    74→    exited: bool = False                        # Whether the process has finished
    75→    exit_code: Optional[int] = None             # Exit code (None if still running)
    76→    output_buffer: str = ""                     # Rolling output (last MAX_OUTPUT_CHARS)
    77→    max_output_chars: int = MAX_OUTPUT_CHARS
    78→    detached: bool = False                      # True if recovered from crash (no pipe)
    79→    _lock: threading.Lock = field(default_factory=threading.Lock)
    80→    _reader_thread: Optional[threading.Thread] = field(default=None, repr=False)
    81→    _pty: Any = field(default=None, repr=False)  # ptyprocess handle (when use_pty=True)
    82→
    83→
    84→class ProcessRegistry:
    85→    """
    86→    In-memory registry of running and finished background processes.
    87→
    88→    Thread-safe. Accessed from:
    89→      - Executor threads (terminal_tool, process tool handlers)
    90→      - Gateway asyncio loop (watcher tasks, session reset checks)
    91→      - Cleanup thread (sandbox reaping coordination)
    92→    """
    93→
    94→    _SHELL_NOISE_SUBSTRINGS = (
    95→        "bash: cannot set terminal process group",
    96→        "bash: no job control in this shell",
    97→        "no job control in this shell",
    98→        "cannot set terminal process group",
    99→        "tcsetattr: Inappropriate ioctl for device",
   100→    )
   101→
   102→    def __init__(self):
   103→        self._running: Dict[str, ProcessSession] = {}
   104→        self._finished: Dict[str, ProcessSession] = {}
   105→        self._lock = threading.Lock()
   106→
   107→        # Side-channel for check_interval watchers (gateway reads after agent run)
   108→        self.pending_watchers: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
   109→
   110→    @staticmethod
   111→    def _clean_shell_noise(text: str) -> str:
   112→        """Strip shell startup warnings from the beginning of output."""
   113→        lines = text.split("\n")
   114→        while lines and any(noise in lines[0] for noise in ProcessRegistry._SHELL_NOISE_SUBSTRINGS):
   115→            lines.pop(0)
   116→        return "\n".join(lines)

   145→            started_at=time.time(),
   146→        )

    33→    print(color("│                         Scheduled Jobs                                  │", Colors.CYAN))
    34→    print(color("└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘", Colors.CYAN))
    35→    print()
    36→    
    37→    for job in jobs:
    38→        job_id = job.get("id", "?")[:8]
    39→        name = job.get("name", "(unnamed)")
    40→        schedule = job.get("schedule_display", job.get("schedule", {}).get("value", "?"))
    41→        enabled = job.get("enabled", True)
    42→        next_run = job.get("next_run_at", "?")
    43→        
    44→        repeat_info = job.get("repeat", {})
    45→        repeat_times = repeat_info.get("times")
    46→        repeat_completed = repeat_info.get("completed", 0)
    47→        
    48→        if repeat_times:
    49→            repeat_str = f"{repeat_completed}/{repeat_times}"
    50→        else:
    51→            repeat_str = "∞"
    52→        
    53→        deliver = job.get("deliver", ["local"])
    54→        if isinstance(deliver, str):
    55→            deliver = [deliver]
    56→        deliver_str = ", ".join(deliver)
    57→        
    58→        if not enabled:
    59→            status = color("[disabled]", Colors.RED)
    60→        else:
    61→            status = color("[active]", Colors.GREEN)
    62→        
    63→        print(f"  {color(job_id, Colors.YELLOW)} {status}")
    64→        print(f"    Name:      {name}")
    65→        print(f"    Schedule:  {schedule}")
    66→        print(f"    Repeat:    {repeat_str}")
    67→        print(f"    Next run:  {next_run}")
    68→        print(f"    Deliver:   {deliver_str}")
    69→        print()
    70→    
    71→    # Warn if gateway isn't running
    72→    from hermes_cli.gateway import find_gateway_pids
    73→    if not find_gateway_pids():
    74→        print(color("  ⚠  Gateway is not running — jobs won't fire automatically.", Colors.YELLOW))
    75→        print(color("     Start it with: hermes gateway install", Colors.DIM))
    76→        print()
    77→
    78→
    79→def cron_tick():
    80→    """Run due jobs once and exit."""
    81→    from cron.scheduler import tick
    82→    tick(verbose=True)
    83→
    84→
    85→def cron_status():
    86→    """Show cron execution status."""
    87→    from cron.jobs import list_jobs
    88→    from hermes_cli.gateway import find_gateway_pids
    89→    
    90→    print()
    91→    
    92→    pids = find_gateway_pids()
    93→    if pids:
    94→        print(color("✓ Gateway is running — cron jobs will fire automatically", Colors.GREEN))
    95→        print(f"  PID: {', '.join(map(str, pids))}")
    96→    else:
    97→        print(color("✗ Gateway is not running — cron jobs will NOT fire", Colors.RED))
    98→        print()
    99→        print("  To enable automatic execution:")
   100→        print("    hermes gateway install    # Install as system service (recommended)")
   101→        print("    hermes gateway            # Or run in foreground")
   102→    
   103→    print()
   104→    
   105→    jobs = list_jobs(include_disabled=False)
   106→    if jobs:
   107→        next_runs = [j.get("next_run_at") for j in jobs if j.get("next_run_at")]
   108→        print(f"  {len(jobs)} active job(s)")
   109→        if next_runs:
   110→            print(f"  Next run: {min(next_runs)}")
   111→    else:
   112→        print("  No active jobs")
   113→    
   114→    print()
   115→
   116→
   117→def cron_command(args):
   118→    """Handle cron subcommands."""
   119→    subcmd = getattr(args, 'cron_command', None)
   120→    
   121→    if subcmd is None or subcmd == "list":
   122→        show_all = getattr(args, 'all', False)
   123→        cron_list(show_all)
   124→    
   125→    elif subcmd == "tick":
   126→        cron_tick()
   127→    
   128→    elif subcmd == "status":
   129→        cron_status()
   130→    
   131→    else:
   132→        print(f"Unknown cron command: {subcmd}")
   133→        print("Usage: hermes cron [list|status|tick]")
   134→        sys.exit(1)
   135→

1→"""
     2→Cron job scheduler - executes due jobs.
     3→
     4→Provides tick() which checks for due jobs and runs them. The gateway
     5→calls this every 60 seconds from a background thread.
     6→
     7→Uses a file-based lock (~/.hermes/cron/.tick.lock) so only one tick
     8→runs at a time if multiple processes overlap.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→import asyncio
    12→import logging
    13→import os
    14→import sys
    15→import traceback
    16→
    17→# fcntl is Unix-only; on Windows use msvcrt for file locking
    18→try:
    19→    import fcntl
    20→except ImportError:
    21→    fcntl = None
    22→    try:
    23→        import msvcrt
    24→    except ImportError:
    25→        msvcrt = None
    26→from datetime import datetime
    27→from pathlib import Path
    28→from typing import Optional
    29→
    30→from hermes_time import now as _hermes_now
    31→
    32→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    33→
    34→# Add parent directory to path for imports

    36→
    37→from cron.jobs import get_due_jobs, mark_job_run, save_job_output
    38→
    39→# Resolve Hermes home directory (respects HERMES_HOME override)
    40→_hermes_home = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes"))
    41→
    42→# File-based lock prevents concurrent ticks from gateway + daemon + systemd timer
    43→_LOCK_DIR = _hermes_home / "cron"
    44→_LOCK_FILE = _LOCK_DIR / ".tick.lock"
    45→
    46→
    47→def _resolve_origin(job: dict) -> Optional[dict]:
    48→    """Extract origin info from a job, preserving any extra routing metadata."""
    49→    origin = job.get("origin")
    50→    if not origin:
    51→        return None
    52→    platform = origin.get("platform")
    53→    chat_id = origin.get("chat_id")
    54→    if platform and chat_id:
    55→        return origin
    56→    return None
    57→
    58→
    59→def _deliver_result(job: dict, content: str) -> None:
    60→    """
    61→    Deliver job output to the configured target (origin chat, specific platform, etc.).
    62→
    63→    Uses the standalone platform send functions from send_message_tool so delivery
    64→    works whether or not the gateway is running.
    65→    """
    66→    deliver = job.get("deliver", "local")
    67→    origin = _resolve_origin(job)
    68→
    69→    if deliver == "local":
    70→        return
    71→
    72→    thread_id = None
    73→
    74→    # Resolve target platform + chat_id
    75→    if deliver == "origin":
    76→        if not origin:
    77→            logger.warning("Job '%s' deliver=origin but no origin stored, skipping delivery", job["id"])
    78→            return
    79→        platform_name = origin["platform"]
    80→        chat_id = origin["chat_id"]
    81→        thread_id = origin.get("thread_id")
    82→    elif ":" in deliver:
    83→        platform_name, chat_id = deliver.split(":", 1)
    84→    else:
    85→        # Bare platform name like "telegram" — need to resolve to origin or home channel
    86→        platform_name = deliver
    87→        if origin and origin.get("platform") == platform_name:
    88→            chat_id = origin["chat_id"]
    89→            thread_id = origin.get("thread_id")
    90→        else:
    91→            # Fall back to home channel
    92→            chat_id = os.getenv(f"{platform_name.upper()}_HOME_CHANNEL", "")
    93→            if not chat_id:
    94→                logger.warning("Job '%s' deliver=%s but no chat_id or home channel. Set via: hermes config set %s_HOME_CHANNEL <channel_id>", job["id"], deliver, platform_name.upper())
    95→                return
    96→
    97→    from tools.send_message_tool import _send_to_platform
    98→    from gateway.config import load_gateway_config, Platform
    99→
   100→    platform_map = {
   101→        "telegram": Platform.TELEGRAM,
   102→        "discord": Platform.DISCORD,
   103→        "slack": Platform.SLACK,
   104→        "whatsapp": Platform.WHATSAPP,
   105→        "signal": Platform.SIGNAL,
   106→        "email": Platform.EMAIL,
   107→    }
   108→    platform = platform_map.get(platform_name.lower())
   109→    if not platform:
   110→        logger.warning("Job '%s': unknown platform '%s' for delivery", job["id"], platform_name)
   111→        return
   112→
   113→    try:
   114→        config = load_gateway_config()
   115→    except Exception as e:
   116→        logger.error("Job '%s': failed to load gateway config for delivery: %s", job["id"], e)
   117→        return
   118→
   119→    pconfig = config.platforms.get(platform)
   120→    if not pconfig or not pconfig.enabled:
   121→        logger.warning("Job '%s': platform '%s' not configured/enabled", job["id"], platform_name)
   122→        return
   123→
   124→    # Run the async send in a fresh event loop (safe from any thread)
   125→    try:
   126→        result = asyncio.run(_send_to_platform(platform, pconfig, chat_id, content, thread_id=thread_id))
   127→    except RuntimeError:
   128→        # asyncio.run() fails if there's already a running loop in this thread;
   129→        # spin up a new thread to avoid that.
   130→        import concurrent.futures
   131→        with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1) as pool:
   132→            future = pool.submit(asyncio.run, _send_to_platform(platform, pconfig, chat_id, content, thread_id=thread_id))
   133→            result = future.result(timeout=30)
   134→    except Exception as e:
   135→        logger.error("Job '%s': delivery to %s:%s failed: %s", job["id"], platform_name, chat_id, e)
   136→        return
   137→
   138→    if result and result.get("error"):
   139→        logger.error("Job '%s': delivery error: %s", job["id"], result["error"])
   140→    else:
   141→        logger.info("Job '%s': delivered to %s:%s", job["id"], platform_name, chat_id)
   142→        # Mirror the delivered content into the target's gateway session
   143→        try:
   144→            from gateway.mirror import mirror_to_session
   145→            mirror_to_session(platform_name, chat_id, content, source_label="cron", thread_id=thread_id)
   146→        except Exception as e:
   147→            logger.warning("Job '%s': mirror_to_session failed: %s", job["id"], e)
   148→
   149→
   150→def run_job(job: dict) -> tuple[bool, str, str, Optional[str]]:

1→"""
     2→Session mirroring for cross-platform message delivery.
     3→
     4→When a message is sent to a platform (via send_message or cron delivery),
     5→this module appends a "delivery-mirror" record to the target session's
     6→transcript so the receiving-side agent has context about what was sent.
     7→
     8→Standalone -- works from CLI, cron, and gateway contexts without needing
     9→the full SessionStore machinery.
    10→"""
    11→
    12→import json
    13→import logging
    14→from datetime import datetime
    15→from pathlib import Path
    16→from typing import Optional
    17→
    18→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    19→
    20→_SESSIONS_DIR = Path.home() / ".hermes" / "sessions"
    21→_SESSIONS_INDEX = _SESSIONS_DIR / "sessions.json"
    22→
    23→
    24→def mirror_to_session(
    25→    platform: str,
    26→    chat_id: str,
    27→    message_text: str,
    28→    source_label: str = "cli",
    29→    thread_id: Optional[str] = None,
    30→) -> bool:
    31→    """
    32→    Append a delivery-mirror message to the target session's transcript.
    33→
    34→    Finds the gateway session that matches the given platform + chat_id,
    35→    then writes a mirror entry to both the JSONL transcript and SQLite DB.
    36→
    37→    Returns True if mirrored successfully, False if no matching session or error.
    38→    All errors are caught -- this is never fatal.
    39→    """
    40→    try:
    41→        session_id = _find_session_id(platform, str(chat_id), thread_id=thread_id)
    42→        if not session_id:
    43→            logger.debug("Mirror: no session found for %s:%s:%s", platform, chat_id, thread_id)
    44→            return False
    45→
    46→        mirror_msg = {
    47→            "role": "assistant",
    48→            "content": message_text,
    49→            "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat(),
    50→            "mirror": True,
    51→            "mirror_source": source_label,
    52→        }
    53→
    54→        _append_to_jsonl(session_id, mirror_msg)
    55→        _append_to_sqlite(session_id, mirror_msg)
    56→
    57→        logger.debug("Mirror: wrote to session %s (from %s)", session_id, source_label)
    58→        return True
    59→
    60→    except Exception as e:
    61→        logger.debug("Mirror failed for %s:%s:%s: %s", platform, chat_id, thread_id, e)
    62→        return False
    63→
    64→
    65→def _find_session_id(platform: str, chat_id: str, thread_id: Optional[str] = None) -> Optional[str]:
    66→    """
    67→    Find the active session_id for a platform + chat_id pair.
    68→
    69→    Scans sessions.json entries and matches where origin.chat_id == chat_id
    70→    on the right platform.  DM session keys don't embed the chat_id
    71→    (e.g. "agent:main:telegram:dm"), so we check the origin dict.
    72→    """
    73→    if not _SESSIONS_INDEX.exists():
    74→        return None
    75→
    76→    try:
    77→        with open(_SESSIONS_INDEX, encoding="utf-8") as f:
    78→            data = json.load(f)
    79→    except Exception:
    80→        return None
    81→
    82→    platform_lower = platform.lower()
    83→    best_match = None
    84→    best_updated = ""
    85→
    86→    for _key, entry in data.items():
    87→        origin = entry.get("origin") or {}
    88→        entry_platform = (origin.get("platform") or entry.get("platform", "")).lower()
    89→
    90→        if entry_platform != platform_lower:
    91→            continue
    92→
    93→        origin_chat_id = str(origin.get("chat_id", ""))
    94→        if origin_chat_id == str(chat_id):
    95→            origin_thread_id = origin.get("thread_id")
    96→            if thread_id is not None and str(origin_thread_id or "") != str(thread_id):
    97→                continue
    98→            updated = entry.get("updated_at", "")
    99→            if updated > best_updated:
   100→                best_updated = updated
   101→                best_match = entry.get("session_id")
   102→
   103→    return best_match
   104→
   105→
   106→def _append_to_jsonl(session_id: str, message: dict) -> None:
   107→    """Append a message to the JSONL transcript file."""
   108→    transcript_path = _SESSIONS_DIR / f"{session_id}.jsonl"
   109→    try:
   110→        with open(transcript_path, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   111→            f.write(json.dumps(message, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
   112→    except Exception as e:
   113→        logger.debug("Mirror JSONL write failed: %s", e)
   114→
   115→
   116→def _append_to_sqlite(session_id: str, message: dict) -> None:
   117→    """Append a message to the SQLite session database."""
   118→    db = None
   119→    try:
   120→        from hermes_state import SessionDB
   121→        db = SessionDB()
   122→        db.append_message(
   123→            session_id=session_id,
   124→            role=message.get("role", "assistant"),
   125→            content=message.get("content"),
   126→        )
   127→    except Exception as e:
   128→        logger.debug("Mirror SQLite write failed: %s", e)
   129→    finally:
   130→        if db is not None:
   131→            db.close()
   132→

1→"""
     2→Gateway runtime status helpers.
     3→
     4→Provides PID-file based detection of whether the gateway daemon is running,
     5→used by send_message's check_fn to gate availability in the CLI.
     6→
     7→The PID file lives at ``{HERMES_HOME}/gateway.pid``.  HERMES_HOME defaults to
     8→``~/.hermes`` but can be overridden via the environment variable.  This means
     9→separate HERMES_HOME directories naturally get separate PID files — a property
    10→that will be useful when we add named profiles (multiple agents running
    11→concurrently under distinct configurations).
    12→"""
    13→
    14→import os
    15→from pathlib import Path
    16→from typing import Optional
    17→
    18→
    19→def _get_pid_path() -> Path:
    20→    """Return the path to the gateway PID file, respecting HERMES_HOME."""
    21→    home = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes"))
    22→    return home / "gateway.pid"
    23→
    24→
    25→def write_pid_file() -> None:
    26→    """Write the current process PID to the gateway PID file."""
    27→    pid_path = _get_pid_path()
    28→    pid_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    29→    pid_path.write_text(str(os.getpid()))
    30→
    31→
    32→def remove_pid_file() -> None:
    33→    """Remove the gateway PID file if it exists."""
    34→    try:
    35→        _get_pid_path().unlink(missing_ok=True)
    36→    except Exception:
    37→        pass
    38→
    39→
    40→def get_running_pid() -> Optional[int]:
    41→    """Return the PID of a running gateway instance, or ``None``.
    42→
    43→    Checks the PID file and verifies the process is actually alive.
    44→    Cleans up stale PID files automatically.
    45→    """
    46→    pid_path = _get_pid_path()
    47→    if not pid_path.exists():
    48→        return None
    49→    try:
    50→        pid = int(pid_path.read_text().strip())
    51→        os.kill(pid, 0)  # signal 0 = existence check, no actual signal sent
    52→        return pid
    53→    except (ValueError, ProcessLookupError, PermissionError):
    54→        # Stale PID file — process is gone
    55→        remove_pid_file()
    56→        return None
    57→
    58→
    59→def is_gateway_running() -> bool:
    60→    """Check if the gateway daemon is currently running."""
    61→    return get_running_pid() is not None
    62→

1→"""
     2→Event Hook System
     3→
     4→A lightweight event-driven system that fires handlers at key lifecycle points.
     5→Hooks are discovered from ~/.hermes/hooks/ directories, each containing:
     6→  - HOOK.yaml  (metadata: name, description, events list)
     7→  - handler.py (Python handler with async def handle(event_type, context))
     8→
     9→Events:
    10→  - gateway:startup     -- Gateway process starts
    11→  - session:start       -- New session created
    12→  - session:reset       -- User ran /new or /reset
    13→  - agent:start         -- Agent begins processing a message
    14→  - agent:step          -- Each turn in the tool-calling loop
    15→  - agent:end           -- Agent finishes processing
    16→  - command:*           -- Any slash command executed (wildcard match)
    17→
    18→Errors in hooks are caught and logged but never block the main pipeline.
    19→"""
    20→
    21→import asyncio
    22→import importlib.util
    23→import os
    24→from pathlib import Path
    25→from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Optional
    26→
    27→import yaml
    28→
    29→
    30→HOOKS_DIR = Path(os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/hooks"))
    31→
    32→
    33→class HookRegistry:
    34→    """
    35→    Discovers, loads, and fires event hooks.
    36→
    37→    Usage:
    38→        registry = HookRegistry()
    39→        registry.discover_and_load()
    40→        await registry.emit("agent:start", {"platform": "telegram", ...})
    41→    """
    42→
    43→    def __init__(self):
    44→        # event_type -> [handler_fn, ...]
    45→        self._handlers: Dict[str, List[Callable]] = {}
    46→        self._loaded_hooks: List[dict] = []  # metadata for listing
    47→
    48→    @property
    49→    def loaded_hooks(self) -> List[dict]:
    50→        """Return metadata about all loaded hooks."""
    51→        return list(self._loaded_hooks)
    52→
    53→    def discover_and_load(self) -> None:
    54→        """
    55→        Scan the hooks directory for hook directories and load their handlers.
    56→
    57→        Each hook directory must contain:
    58→          - HOOK.yaml with at least 'name' and 'events' keys
    59→          - handler.py with a top-level 'handle' function (sync or async)
    60→        """
    61→        if not HOOKS_DIR.exists():
    62→            return
    63→
    64→        for hook_dir in sorted(HOOKS_DIR.iterdir()):
    65→            if not hook_dir.is_dir():
    66→                continue
    67→
    68→            manifest_path = hook_dir / "HOOK.yaml"
    69→            handler_path = hook_dir / "handler.py"
    70→
    71→            if not manifest_path.exists() or not handler_path.exists():
    72→                continue
    73→
    74→            try:
    75→                manifest = yaml.safe_load(manifest_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    76→                if not manifest or not isinstance(manifest, dict):
    77→                    print(f"[hooks] Skipping {hook_dir.name}: invalid HOOK.yaml", flush=True)
    78→                    continue
    79→
    80→                hook_name = manifest.get("name", hook_dir.name)
    81→                events = manifest.get("events", [])
    82→                if not events:
    83→                    print(f"[hooks] Skipping {hook_name}: no events declared", flush=True)
    84→                    continue
    85→
    86→                # Dynamically load the handler module
    87→                spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(
    88→                    f"hermes_hook_{hook_name}", handler_path
    89→                )
    90→                if spec is None or spec.loader is None:
    91→                    print(f"[hooks] Skipping {hook_name}: could not load handler.py", flush=True)
    92→                    continue
    93→
    94→                module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    95→                spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    96→
    97→                handle_fn = getattr(module, "handle", None)
    98→                if handle_fn is None:

     2→Channel directory -- cached map of reachable channels/contacts per platform.

     4→Built on gateway startup, refreshed periodically (every 5 min), and saved to

    20→def _session_entry_id(origin: Dict[str, Any]) -> Optional[str]:
    21→    chat_id = origin.get("chat_id")
    22→    if not chat_id:
    23→        return None
    24→    thread_id = origin.get("thread_id")
    25→    if thread_id:
    26→        return f"{chat_id}:{thread_id}"
    27→    return str(chat_id)
    28→
    29→
    30→def _session_entry_name(origin: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    31→    base_name = origin.get("chat_name") or origin.get("user_name") or str(origin.get("chat_id"))
    32→    thread_id = origin.get("thread_id")
    33→    if not thread_id:
    34→        return base_name
    35→
    36→    topic_label = origin.get("chat_topic") or f"topic {thread_id}"
    37→    return f"{base_name} / {topic_label}"
    38→
    39→
    40→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    41→# Build / refresh
    42→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    43→
    44→def build_channel_directory(adapters: Dict[Any, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    45→    """
    46→    Build a channel directory from connected platform adapters and session data.
    47→
    48→    Returns the directory dict and writes it to DIRECTORY_PATH.
    49→    """
    50→    from gateway.config import Platform
    51→
    52→    platforms: Dict[str, List[Dict[str, str]]] = {}
    53→
    54→    for platform, adapter in adapters.items():
    55→        try:
    56→            if platform == Platform.DISCORD:
    57→                platforms["discord"] = _build_discord(adapter)
    58→            elif platform == Platform.SLACK:
    59→                platforms["slack"] = _build_slack(adapter)
    60→        except Exception as e:
    61→            logger.warning("Channel directory: failed to build %s: %s", platform.value, e)
    62→
    63→    # Telegram, WhatsApp & Signal can't enumerate chats -- pull from session history
    64→    for plat_name in ("telegram", "whatsapp", "signal", "email"):
    65→        if plat_name not in platforms:
    66→            platforms[plat_name] = _build_from_sessions(plat_name)
    67→
    68→    directory = {
    69→        "updated_at": datetime.now().isoformat(),
    70→        "platforms": platforms,
    71→    }
    72→
    73→    try:
    74→        DIRECTORY_PATH.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    75→        with open(DIRECTORY_PATH, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    76→            json.dump(directory, f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)
    77→    except Exception as e:

    80→    return directory

    99→                "name": ch.name,

# Hermes Long-Running Server Infrastructure Analysis

## 1. GATEWAY RUNNER - Main Server Component

Location: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py (3,866 lines)

### Core Class: GatewayRunner
- Manages lifecycle of all platform adapters (Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Email)
- Handles session management and message routing
- Tracks running AIAgent instances per session
- Main entry point: `async def start_gateway(config=None, replace=False)`

### Session Management
- **SessionStore**: In-memory registry mapping session_key -> SessionEntry
  - Location: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/session.py (812 lines)
  - Persistent storage: ~/.hermes/sessions/sessions.json (index) + SQLite SessionDB
  - Session keys: Format like "agent:main:platform:chat_id" or "platform:user_id:chat_id"
  - Session ID format: "YYYYmmdd_HHMMSS_xxxxxxxx" (timestamp + uuid)
  - Handles reset policies: idle-based, daily-based, or none

- **SessionEntry**: Stores session_id, platform, chat_id, user_id, chat_type, timestamp
- **SessionSource**: Metadata about where messages come from

### Running Agent Registry
- `self._running_agents: Dict[str, Any]` maps session_key -> AIAgent instance
- Used for interrupt support (stopping long-running agents when user sends new message)
- Agents are temporary instances, created per-message
- Persistent Honcho managers stored per session_key: `self._honcho_managers`

### Platform Adapters
Location: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/platforms/

All inherit from BasePlatformAdapter:
- base.py: Abstract base with MessageEvent, send(), send_typing(), send_image(), etc.
- telegram.py, discord.py, slack.py, whatsapp.py, signal.py, email.py
- Each adapter: async receive loop + message routing to _handle_message()
- MessageEvent: Contains source (SessionSource), text, type (text/image/etc), timestamp

### Command Processing Pipeline (_handle_message)
1. Authorization check (user allowlist)
2. Priority interrupt (if agent running for session, interrupt it immediately)
3. Command parsing (/new, /reset, /help, /status, /model, /provider, etc.)
4. Session lookup/creation via SessionStore
5. Build SessionContext prompt injection
6. Create AIAgent instance with session_id
7. Run agent.run_conversation()
8. Store response in transcript

## 2. AIAgent INSTANTIATION AND MANAGEMENT

Location: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/run_agent.py (281,916 bytes)

### Class: AIAgent
Created fresh per-message in gateway with:
- session_id: For logging/tracking
- platform: "telegram", "discord", etc. for context injection
- honcho_session_key: For persistent memory across sessions
- honcho_manager: Shared HonchoSessionManager (persistent per gateway session_key)
- ephemeral_system_prompt: Injected context from gateway
- model, provider, base_url: Model selection
- Callbacks: tool_progress_callback, thinking_callback, step_callback

### Agent Lifecycle
1. Created with config from gateway
2. run_conversation(messages) executes tool loop
3. Tools make changes, read files, etc.
4. Agent returns final response
5. Destroyed after response (memory flushed to Honcho if configured)

## 3. PROCESS REGISTRY - Background Process Tracking

Location: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/tools/process_registry.py (700+ lines)

### Global Registry
- ProcessRegistry singleton: process_registry
- Tracks spawned background processes (command_id -> ProcessSession)
- Output buffering (200KB rolling window)
- Status polling, wait with timeout, kill support
- Session-keyed tracking for reset protection

### Integration with Gateway
- Gateway checks active processes before session reset
- Protected from reset while processes running
- Checkpoint file: ~/.hermes/processes.json for crash recovery

## 4. CRON SCHEDULER - Background Job System

Location: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cron/

### Jobs Database
- ~hermes/cron/jobs.json: Scheduled jobs with:
  - schedule (cron expression)
  - command/prompt to execute
  - delivery target (origin, platform, local)
  - origin metadata (platform, chat_id, user_id)
  - repeat count, enabled flag

### Scheduler (scheduler.py)
- tick() function: Checks for due jobs, runs them, saves output
- Runs every 60 seconds from background thread in gateway
- Uses file-based lock to prevent concurrent ticks
- Delivery via _send_to_platform (uses send_message_tool)

### Integration with Gateway
- Gateway spawns _start_cron_ticker() thread at startup
- Runs tick() on 60-second interval
- Also refreshes channel directory every 5 minutes
- Automatic image cache cleanup every hour

## 5. SESSION MIRRORING - Cross-Platform Delivery

Location: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/mirror.py

- Mirror function sends cron outputs to agent sessions
- Looks up session_id from platform + chat_id
- Appends delivery-mirror entry to JSONL transcript + SQLite DB
- Used by cron, send_message_tool, and other delivery

## 6. CHANNEL DIRECTORY - Reachable Contacts/Channels

Location: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/channel_directory.py

- Cached directory at ~/.hermes/channel_directory.json
- Built from platform adapters + session history
- Discord: Enumerate text channels via discord.py client
- Slack: From socket mode client
- Telegram/WhatsApp/Signal/Email: From sessions.json origin data
- Refreshed every 5 minutes by cron ticker
- Used by send_message tool for name -> ID resolution

## 7. SERVICE MANAGEMENT - systemd/launchd Integration

Location: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/gateway.py

### Service Definitions
- Linux: ~/.config/systemd/user/hermes-gateway.service
  - ExecStart: python -m hermes_cli.main gateway run --replace
  - Restart=on-failure, RestartSec=10
  - StandardOutput/StandardError to journald
  
- macOS: ~/Library/LaunchAgents/ai.hermes.gateway.plist
  - RunAtLoad=true (starts on login)
  - KeepAlive with SuccessfulExit=false (respawn on crash)
  - Logs to ~/.hermes/logs/gateway.{log,error.log}

### Process Management
- find_gateway_pids(): Finds running gateway processes via ps aux
- kill_gateway_processes(): Sends SIGTERM to gateways
- PID file: ~/.hermes/gateway.pid for duplicate-instance guard
- CLI commands: start, stop, restart, status, install, uninstall

## 8. EVENT HOOK SYSTEM - Extensibility

Location: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/hooks.py

- Hook directories at ~/.hermes/hooks/
- Each hook: HOOK.yaml (metadata) + handler.py (async handle function)
- Events:
  - gateway:startup
  - session:start, session:reset
  - agent:start, agent:step, agent:end
  - command:* (wildcard for slash commands)
- Non-blocking: Errors caught and logged

## 9. LOGGING AND MONITORING

- File logs: ~/.hermes/logs/gateway.log (rotating, 5MB max, 3 backups)
- Error logs: ~/.hermes/logs/errors.log (warnings/errors only)
- Log format: %(asctime)s %(levelname)s %(name)s: %(message)s
- Redacting formatter strips sensitive data
- Session transcripts: ~/.hermes/sessions/{session_id}.jsonl (JSONL format)
- SQLite DB: hermes_state.SessionDB for message storage

## 10. RUNTIME CONFIGURATION

Location: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/config.py

### GatewayConfig (YAML)
- Messaging platform credentials (TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN, DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN, etc.)
- Session reset policies per platform/chat_type
- Provider routing and fallback models
- Reasoning config, system prompt overrides
- Loaded from ~/.hermes/config.yaml or environment variables

## 11. WHAT'S NOT THERE (Important Absence)

- NO HTTP REST API endpoints (no Flask/FastAPI)
- NO WebSocket server
- NO IPC sockets
- NO pub/sub message broker
- NO admin control panel
- NO inter-process command channel

All control/communication is:
1. Platform-based (Telegram DMs, Discord DMs, etc. commands)
2. File-based (sessions.json, cron/jobs.json, channel_directory.json)
3. Process-based (systemd/launchd for service management)
4. CLI-based (hermes CLI commands)

## 12. EXPANSION POINTS FOR YOUR USE CASE

To add control channels for programmatic access:
1. Could add HTTP REST API in gateway (FastAPI) on Unix socket
2. Could add gRPC service for inter-agent communication
3. Could create new custom platform adapter (inherits BasePlatformAdapter)
4. Could use hook system to listen for events
5. Could use send_message tool from CLI to trigger gateway actions
```

## Summary: Hermes Server Infrastructure Analysis

I've completed a thorough investigation of Hermes' long-running server infrastructure. Here's what exists:

### **CORE INFRASTRUCTURE**

**1. Gateway Runner** (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py`)
- 3,866-line async server managing messaging platform integrations
- Main class: `GatewayRunner` - runs via `async def start_gateway()`
- No HTTP server (Flask/FastAPI) - purely event-driven architecture
- Multi-platform support: Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Email

**2. Session Registry & Management** (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/session.py`)
- **SessionStore**: In-memory dictionary mapping session_key -> SessionEntry
  - Session keys: `"agent:main:telegram:chat_id"` or `"platform:user_id:chat_id"`
  - Persistent storage at `~/.hermes/sessions/sessions.json` (JSON index)
  - Also uses SQLite DB (`hermes_state.SessionDB`) for message history
  - Session IDs: `"20240314_031234_a1b2c3d4"` (timestamp + uuid)

**3. AIAgent Instance Tracking** (within `GatewayRunner`)
- `self._running_agents: Dict[str, AIAgent]` - Maps session_key to active agent
- Agents are **temporary**, created per-message via `AIAgent(...)`
- Each agent gets injected context: session_id, platform, ephemeral_system_prompt
- Persistent Honcho managers stored in `self._honcho_managers[session_key]`

**4. Process Registry** (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/tools/process_registry.py`)
- Global singleton: `process_registry`
- Tracks background processes spawned via `terminal(background=true)`
- In-memory dict: `_running` and `_finished` (ProcessSession objects)
- Output buffering (200KB rolling window)
- Session-keyed tracking prevents reset during active background tasks
- Checkpoint: `~/.hermes/processes.json` for crash recovery

### **ASYNC MESSAGING PIPELINE**

Platform adapters inherit from `BasePlatformAdapter` (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/platforms/base.py`):
- Each platform runs async receive loop
- Receives `MessageEvent(source, text, type, timestamp)`
- Routes to `GatewayRunner._handle_message(event)`:
  1. Authorization check (allowlist)
  2. **Priority interrupt** - if agent running for session, interrupt immediately
  3. Command parsing (/new, /reset, /model, /provider, etc.)
  4. Session lookup/creation
  5. Create AIAgent instance
  6. Execute `agent.run_conversation()`
  7. Store transcript to JSONL + SQLite

### **BACKGROUND JOB SYSTEM**

**Cron Scheduler** (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cron/scheduler.py`)
- Jobs stored: `~/.hermes/cron/jobs.json`
- Background thread spawned at gateway startup: `_start_cron_ticker()`
- Calls `cron_tick()` every 60 seconds
- Execution with file-based lock to prevent concurrent ticks
- Delivery via `_send_to_platform()` (same as send_message tool)
- Also refreshes channel directory every 5 minutes, cleans image cache hourly

**Session Mirroring** (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/mirror.py`)
- After cron delivers output, appends delivery-mirror entry to agent session
- Looks up session_id from platform + chat_id, appends to JSONL + SQLite

### **SERVICE MANAGEMENT**

**CLI Integration** (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/gateway.py`)
- `hermes gateway run` - foreground execution
- `hermes gateway install` - installs systemd (Linux) or launchd (macOS)
- `hermes gateway start/stop/restart/status`
- PID file: `~/.hermes/gateway.pid` (duplicate-instance guard)
- Process detection: `find_gateway_pids()` via ps aux

**systemd** (Linux):
- Path: `~/.config/systemd/user/hermes-gateway.service`
- ExecStart: `python -m hermes_cli.main gateway run --replace`
- Restart=on-failure, RestartSec=10s, KillMode=mixed

**launchd** (macOS):
- Path: `~/Library/LaunchAgents/ai.hermes.gateway.plist`
- RunAtLoad=true, KeepAlive respawning enabled
- Logs to `~/.hermes/logs/gateway.{log,error.log}`

### **EXTENSIBILITY POINTS**

**Hook System** (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/hooks.py`)
- Hook discovery: `~/.hermes/hooks/{hook_name}/`
- Each hook: `HOOK.yaml` (metadata) + `handler.py` (async handle function)
- Events: `gateway:startup`, `session:start`, `session:reset`, `agent:start`, `agent:step`, `agent:end`, `command:*`
- Non-blocking execution

**Channel Directory** (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/channel_directory.py`)
- Cached: `~/.hermes/channel_directory.json`
- Built from platform adapters + session history
- Discord enumerates channels via `discord.py` client
- Telegram/WhatsApp/Signal pull from sessions.json origin data

### **WHAT'S MISSING (Critical)**

- **NO HTTP REST API** (no Flask, FastAPI, or aiohttp server)
- **NO WebSocket endpoints**
- **NO IPC sockets** (Unix domain or otherwise)
- **NO pub/sub message broker**
- **NO admin control endpoints**

All control is via: platform commands (Telegram DMs), file-based config, process signals, and CLI.

### **Key File Locations**

| Component | File | Size |
|-----------|------|------|
| Gateway Runner | `gateway/run.py` | 3,866 lines |
| Session Management | `gateway/session.py` | 812 lines |
| AIAgent Core | `run_agent.py` | 281 KB |
| Process Registry | `tools/process_registry.py` | 700+ lines |
| Cron Scheduler | `cron/scheduler.py` | 13,898 bytes |
| Session Mirroring | `gateway/mirror.py` | 132 lines |
| Platform Adapters | `gateway/platforms/{base,telegram,discord,slack,...}.py` | - |
| Service CLI | `hermes_cli/gateway.py` | 1,075 lines |

This is a **platform-driven, event-based async architecture** with no built-in HTTP/IPC server. To add programmatic control, you'd need to introduce FastAPI/gRPC or create a custom platform adapter.

1→# Desloppify - an agent harness to make your codebase 🤌
     2→
     3→[![PyPI version](https://img.shields.io/pypi/v/desloppify)](https://pypi.org/project/desloppify/) ![Python 3.11+](https://img.shields.io/badge/python-3.11%2B-blue)
     4→
     5→Desloppify gives your AI coding agent the tools to identify, understand, and systematically improve codebase quality. It combines mechanical detection (dead code, duplication, complexity) with subjective LLM review (naming, abstractions, module boundaries), then works through a prioritized fix loop. State persists across scans so it chips away over multiple sessions, and the scoring is designed to resist gaming.
     6→
     7→<img src="assets/explained.png" width="100%">
     8→
     9→The score gives your agent a north-star, and the tooling helps it plan, execute, and resolve issues until it hits your target — with a lot of tricks to keep it on track. A score above 98 should correlate with a codebase a seasoned engineer would call beautiful.
    10→
    11→That score generates a scorecard badge for your GitHub profile or README:
    12→
    13→<img src="assets/scorecard.png" width="100%">
    14→
    15→Currently supports 29 languages — full plugin depth for TypeScript, Python, C#, C++, Dart, GDScript, Go, and Rust; generic linter + tree-sitter support for Ruby, Java, Kotlin, and 18 more. For C++ projects, `compile_commands.json` is the primary analysis path and `Makefile` repositories fall back to best-effort local include scanning.
    16→
    17→## For your agent's consideration...
    18→
    19→Paste this prompt into your agent:
    20→
    21→```
    22→I want you to improve the quality of this codebase. To do this, install and run desloppify.
    23→Run ALL of the following (requires Python 3.11+):
    24→
    25→pip install --upgrade "desloppify[full]"
    26→desloppify update-skill claude    # installs the full workflow guide — pick yours: claude, cursor, codex, copilot, windsurf, gemini
    27→
    28→Add .desloppify/ to your .gitignore — it contains local state that shouldn't be committed.
    29→
    30→Before scanning, check for directories that should be excluded (vendor, build output,
    31→generated code, worktrees, etc.) and exclude obvious ones with `desloppify exclude <path>`.
    32→Share any questionable candidates with me before excluding.
    33→
    34→desloppify scan --path .
    35→desloppify next
    36→
    37→--path is the directory to scan (use "." for the whole project, or "src/" etc).
    38→
    39→Your goal is to get the strict score as high as possible. The scoring resists gaming — the
    40→only way to improve it is to actually make the code better.
    41→
    42→THE LOOP: run `next`. It is the execution queue from the living plan, not the whole backlog.
    43→It tells you what to fix now, which file, and the resolve command to run when done.
    44→Fix it, resolve it, run `next` again. Over and over. This is your main job.
    45→
    46→Use `desloppify backlog` only when you need to inspect broader open work that is not currently
    47→driving execution.
    48→
    49→Don't be lazy. Large refactors and small detailed fixes — do both with equal energy. No task
    50→is too big or too small. Fix things properly, not minimally.
    51→
    52→Use `plan` / `plan queue` to reorder priorities or cluster related issues. Rescan periodically.
    53→The scan output includes agent instructions — follow them, don't substitute your own analysis.
    54→```
    55→
    56→## From Vibe Coding to Vibe Engineering
    57→
    58→Vibe coding gets things built fast. But the codebases it produces tend to rot in ways that are hard to see and harder to fix — not just the mechanical stuff like dead imports, but the structural kind. Abstractions that made sense at first stop making sense. Naming drifts. Error handling is done three different ways. The codebase works, but working in it gets worse over time.
    59→
    60→LLMs are actually good at spotting this now, if you ask them the right questions. That's the core bet here — that an agent with the right framework can hold a codebase to a real standard, the kind that used to require a senior engineer paying close attention over months.
    61→
    62→So we're trying to define what "good" looks like as a score that's actually worth optimizing. Not a lint score you game to 100 by suppressing warnings. Something where improving the number means the codebase genuinely got better. That's hard, and we're not done, but the anti-gaming stuff matters to us a lot — it's the difference between a useful signal and a vanity metric.
    63→
    64→The hope is that anyone can use this to build something a seasoned engineer would look at and respect. That's the bar we're aiming for.
    65→
    66→If you'd like to join a community of vibe engineers who want to build beautiful things, [come hang out](https://discord.gg/aZdzbZrHaY).
    67→
    68→<img src="assets/engineering.png" width="100%">
    69→
    70→---
    71→
    72→Issues, improvements, and PRs are hugely appreciated — [github.com/peteromallet/desloppify](https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify).
    73→
    74→MIT License
    75→

1→"""CLI entry point: parse args, load shared context, dispatch command handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→import logging
     7→import sys
     8→from collections.abc import Mapping
     9→from functools import lru_cache
    10→from pathlib import Path
    11→from typing import Any
    12→
    13→from desloppify.app.cli_support.parser import create_parser as _create_parser
    14→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import resolve_lang
    15→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.command_runtime import CommandRuntime
    16→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
    17→from desloppify.app.commands.registry import CommandHandler, get_command_handlers
    18→from desloppify.base.config import load_config
    19→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import set_exclusions
    20→from desloppify.base.exception_sets import CommandError
    21→from desloppify.base.output.fallbacks import log_best_effort_failure
    22→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    23→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_default_scan_path, get_project_root
    24→from desloppify.base.registry import detector_names, on_detector_registered
    25→from desloppify.base.runtime_state import runtime_scope
    26→from desloppify.languages import available_langs
    27→from desloppify.state_io import load_state
    28→
    29→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    30→
    31→
    32→class _DetectorNamesCacheCompat:
    33→    """Compat shim for tests that poke the legacy detector-name cache."""
    34→
    35→    def __init__(self) -> None:
    36→        self._store: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    37→
    38→    def __contains__(self, key: object) -> bool:
    39→        return key in self._store
    40→
    41→    def __getitem__(self, key: str) -> list[str]:
    42→        return self._store[key]
    43→
    44→    def __setitem__(self, key: str, value: list[str]) -> None:
    45→        self._store[key] = value
    46→
    47→    def pop(self, key: str, default: list[str] | None = None) -> list[str] | None:
    48→        return self._store.pop(key, default)
    49→
    50→
    51→_DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE = _DetectorNamesCacheCompat()
    52→
    53→
    54→@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
    55→def _get_detector_names_cached() -> tuple[str, ...]:
    56→    """Compute detector names once until cache invalidation."""
    57→    return tuple(detector_names())
    58→
    59→
    60→def _get_detector_names() -> list[str]:
    61→    """Return cached detector names, computing on first access."""
    62→    return list(_get_detector_names_cached())
    63→
    64→
    65→def _invalidate_detector_names_cache() -> None:
    66→    """Invalidate detector-name cache when runtime registrations change."""
    67→    _get_detector_names_cached.cache_clear()
    68→    _DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE.pop("names", None)
    69→
    70→
    71→on_detector_registered(_invalidate_detector_names_cache)
    72→
    73→
    74→def create_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
    75→    """Return the top-level argparse parser."""
    76→    return _create_parser(langs=available_langs(), detector_names=_get_detector_names())
    77→
    78→
    79→def _apply_persisted_exclusions(
    80→    args: argparse.Namespace,
    81→    config: Mapping[str, Any],
    82→) -> None:
    83→    """Merge CLI --exclude with persisted config.exclude and apply globally."""
    84→    cli_exclusions = getattr(args, "exclude", None) or []
    85→    persisted_raw = config.get("exclude", [])
    86→    persisted = (
    87→        [entry for entry in persisted_raw if isinstance(entry, str)]
    88→        if isinstance(persisted_raw, list)
    89→        else []
    90→    )
    91→    combined = list(cli_exclusions) + [e for e in persisted if e not in cli_exclusions]
    92→    if not combined:
    93→        return
    94→    set_exclusions(combined)
    95→    if cli_exclusions:
    96→        print(
    97→            colorize(f"  Excluding: {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"),
    98→            file=sys.stderr,
    99→        )
   100→        return
   101→    print(
   102→        colorize(
   103→            f"  Excluding (from config): {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"
   104→        ),
   105→        file=sys.stderr,
   106→    )
   107→
   108→
   109→def _project_root_from_state_path(state_path_value: str | Path | None) -> Path | None:
   110→    """Infer project root from an explicit state path under ``.desloppify``."""
   111→    if state_path_value in (None, ""):
   112→        return None
   113→    try:
   114→        state_file = Path(state_path_value).resolve()
   115→    except OSError:
   116→        return None
   117→    if state_file.parent.name != ".desloppify":
   118→        return None
   119→    if state_file.name == "state.json" or (
   120→        state_file.name.startswith("state-") and state_file.suffix == ".json"
   121→    ):
   122→        return state_file.parent.parent
   123→    return None
   124→
   125→
   126→def _resolve_default_path(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   127→    """Fill args.path from detected language or default source path.
   128→
   129→    For the review command, the last scan path (stored in state) is used as the
   130→    default so that ``desloppify review --prepare`` works on the same scope as
   131→    the preceding scan even when the project files are not under ``src/``.
   132→    """
   133→    if getattr(args, "path", None) is not None:
   134→        return
   135→    runtime_root = get_project_root()
   136→    if getattr(args, "command", None) == "review":
   137→        try:
   138→            state_file = state_path(args)
   139→            if state_file:
   140→                saved = load_state(state_file)
   141→                saved_path = saved.get("scan_path")
   142→                if saved_path:
   143→                    args.path = str((runtime_root / saved_path).resolve())
   144→                    return
   145→        except (OSError, KeyError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError) as exc:
   146→            log_best_effort_failure(logger, "resolve default review path from saved state", exc)
   147→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
   148→    args.path = str(
   149→        get_default_scan_path(
   150→            project_root=runtime_root,
   151→            default_src=lang.default_src if lang else None,
   152→        )
   153→    )
   154→
   155→
   156→def _load_shared_runtime(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   157→    """Load config/state and attach shared objects to parsed args."""
   158→    config = load_config()
   159→
   160→    state_file = state_path(args)
   161→    state = load_state(state_file)
   162→    _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config)
   163→
   164→    args.runtime = CommandRuntime(config=config, state=state, state_path=state_file)
   165→
   166→
   167→def _looks_like_desloppify_checkout(root: Path) -> bool:
   168→    """Return True when *root* appears to be the desloppify source checkout."""
   169→    package_dir = root / "desloppify"
   170→    pyproject = root / "pyproject.toml"
   171→    if not package_dir.is_dir() or not (package_dir / "__init__.py").is_file():
   172→        return False
   173→    if not pyproject.is_file():
   174→        return False
   175→    try:
   176→        text = pyproject.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   177→    except OSError:
   178→        return False
   179→    return 'name = "desloppify"' in text or "name='desloppify'" in text
   180→
   181→
   182→def _running_installed_package_from_checkout(
   183→    *,
   184→    cwd_root: Path | None = None,
   185→    module_file: str | Path | None = None,
   186→) -> bool:
   187→    """Detect running an installed package while standing in a repo checkout."""
   188→    root = (cwd_root or get_project_root()).resolve()
   189→    if not _looks_like_desloppify_checkout(root):
   190→        return False
   191→    current_module = Path(module_file or __file__).resolve()
   192→    try:
   193→        current_module.relative_to(root)
   194→        return False
   195→    except ValueError:
   196→        return True
   197→
   198→
   199→def _warn_if_running_installed_package_from_checkout() -> None:
   200→    """Emit a warning when CLI resolution bypasses the current checkout."""
   201→    if not _running_installed_package_from_checkout():
   202→        return
   203→    root = get_project_root().resolve()
   204→    print(
   205→        colorize(
   206→            "  WARNING: running installed desloppify package while current directory "
   207→            "looks like the desloppify checkout.",
   208→            "yellow",
   209→        ),
   210→        file=sys.stderr,
   211→    )
   212→    print(
   213→        colorize(
   214→            f"  Current checkout: {root}",
   215→            "dim",
   216→        ),
   217→        file=sys.stderr,
   218→    )
   219→    print(
   220→        colorize(
   221→            "  Use `python -m desloppify ...` or `./.venv/bin/desloppify ...` here "
   222→            "to run the local checkout instead of the installed package.",
   223→            "dim",
   224→        ),
   225→        file=sys.stderr,
   226→    )
   227→
   228→
   229→def _resolve_handler(command: str) -> CommandHandler:
   230→    """Resolve a CLI command name to its typed handler callable."""
   231→    return get_command_handlers()[command]
   232→
   233→
   234→def _handle_help_command(
   235→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   236→    parser: argparse.ArgumentParser,
   237→) -> None:
   238→    """Handle explicit help command when present in parser config."""
   239→    topic = list(getattr(args, "topic", []) or [])
   240→    try:
   241→        parser.parse_args([*topic, "--help"])
   242→    except SystemExit:
   243→        return
   244→
   245→
   246→def main() -> None:
   247→    # Ensure Unicode output works on Windows terminals (cp1252 etc.)
   248→    for stream in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr):
   249→        if hasattr(stream, "reconfigure"):
   250→            try:
   251→                stream.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
   252→            except (AttributeError, OSError):
   253→                logger.debug(
   254→                    "Skipping stream reconfigure for %s (not supported)",
   255→                    getattr(stream, "name", "<stream>"),
   256→                )
   257→
   258→    parser = create_parser()
   259→    args = parser.parse_args()
   260→    if not args.command:
   261→        parser.print_help()
   262→        return
   263→    if args.command == "help":
   264→        _handle_help_command(args, parser)
   265→        return
   266→
   267→    try:
   268→        with runtime_scope() as runtime:
   269→            inferred = _project_root_from_state_path(getattr(args, "state", None))
   270→            if inferred is not None:
   271→                runtime.project_root = inferred
   272→            _warn_if_running_installed_package_from_checkout()
   273→            _resolve_default_path(args)
   274→            _load_shared_runtime(args)
   275→
   276→            handler = _resolve_handler(args.command)
   277→            handler(args)
   278→    except CommandError as exc:
   279→        print(colorize(f"  {exc.message}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   280→        sys.exit(exc.exit_code)
   281→    except KeyboardInterrupt:
   282→        print("\nInterrupted.")
   283→        sys.exit(1)
   284→
   285→
   286→if __name__ == "__main__":
   287→    main()
   288→

1→"""Central command registry for CLI command handler resolution."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→from collections.abc import Callable
     7→from functools import lru_cache
     8→
     9→CommandHandler = Callable[[argparse.Namespace], None]
    10→
    11→
    12→def _build_handlers() -> dict[str, CommandHandler]:
    13→    """Import all command modules and build the handler dict on first access."""
    14→    from desloppify.app.commands.autofix import cmd_autofix
    15→    from desloppify.app.commands.backlog import cmd_backlog
    16→    from desloppify.app.commands.config import cmd_config
    17→    from desloppify.app.commands.detect import cmd_detect
    18→    from desloppify.app.commands.directives import cmd_directives
    19→    from desloppify.app.commands.dev import cmd_dev
    20→    from desloppify.app.commands.exclude import cmd_exclude
    21→    from desloppify.app.commands.langs import cmd_langs
    22→    from desloppify.app.commands.move import cmd_move
    23→    from desloppify.app.commands.next import cmd_next
    24→    from desloppify.app.commands.plan import cmd_plan
    25→    from desloppify.app.commands.suppress import cmd_suppress
    26→    from desloppify.app.commands.review import cmd_review
    27→    from desloppify.app.commands.scan import cmd_scan
    28→    from desloppify.app.commands.show import cmd_show
    29→    from desloppify.app.commands.status import cmd_status
    30→    from desloppify.app.commands.update_skill import cmd_update_skill
    31→    from desloppify.app.commands.viz import cmd_tree, cmd_viz
    32→    from desloppify.app.commands.zone import cmd_zone
    33→
    34→    return {
    35→        "scan": cmd_scan,
    36→        "status": cmd_status,
    37→        "show": cmd_show,
    38→        "next": cmd_next,
    39→        "backlog": cmd_backlog,
    40→        "suppress": cmd_suppress,
    41→        "exclude": cmd_exclude,
    42→        "autofix": cmd_autofix,
    43→        "plan": cmd_plan,
    44→        "detect": cmd_detect,
    45→        "tree": cmd_tree,
    46→        "viz": cmd_viz,
    47→        "move": cmd_move,
    48→        "zone": cmd_zone,
    49→        "review": cmd_review,
    50→        "config": cmd_config,
    51→        "directives": cmd_directives,
    52→        "dev": cmd_dev,
    53→        "langs": cmd_langs,
    54→        "update-skill": cmd_update_skill,
    55→    }
    56→
    57→
    58→@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
    59→def get_command_handlers() -> dict[str, CommandHandler]:
    60→    """Return cached command handler dict, building on first access."""
    61→    return _build_handlers()
    62→
    63→
    64→__all__ = ["CommandHandler", "get_command_handlers"]
    65→

1→"""Skill-document versioning and install metadata helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→from dataclasses import dataclass
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
     9→
    10→# Bump this integer whenever docs/SKILL.md changes in a way that agents
    11→# should pick up (new commands, changed workflows, removed sections).
    12→SKILL_VERSION = 6
    13→
    14→SKILL_VERSION_RE = re.compile(r"<!--\s*desloppify-skill-version:\s*(\d+)\s*-->")
    15→SKILL_OVERLAY_RE = re.compile(r"<!--\s*desloppify-overlay:\s*(\w+)\s*-->")
    16→
    17→SKILL_BEGIN = "<!-- desloppify-begin -->"
    18→SKILL_END = "<!-- desloppify-end -->"
    19→
    20→# Locations where the skill doc might be installed, relative to project root.
    21→SKILL_SEARCH_PATHS = (
    22→    ".agents/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md",
    23→    ".claude/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md",
    24→    ".opencode/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md",
    25→    "AGENTS.md",
    26→    "CLAUDE.md",
    27→    ".cursor/rules/desloppify.md",
    28→    ".github/copilot-instructions.md",
    29→)
    30→
    31→# Interface name → (target file, overlay filename, dedicated).
    32→# Dedicated files are overwritten entirely; shared files get section replacement.
    33→SKILL_TARGETS: dict[str, tuple[str, str, bool]] = {
    34→    "amp": (".agents/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md", "AMP", True),
    35→    "claude": (".claude/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md", "CLAUDE", True),
    36→    # OpenCode support added with thanks to @H3xKatana.
    37→    "opencode": (".opencode/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md", "OPENCODE", True),
    38→    "codex": (".agents/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md", "CODEX", True),
    39→    "cursor": (".cursor/rules/desloppify.md", "CURSOR", True),
    40→    "copilot": (".github/copilot-instructions.md", "COPILOT", False),
    41→    "windsurf": ("AGENTS.md", "WINDSURF", False),
    42→    "gemini": ("AGENTS.md", "GEMINI", False),
    43→    "hermes": ("AGENTS.md", "HERMES", False),
    44→}
    45→
    46→
    47→@dataclass
    48→class SkillInstall:
    49→    """Detected skill document installation."""
    50→
    51→    rel_path: str
    52→    version: int
    53→    overlay: str | None
    54→    stale: bool
    55→
    56→
    57→def find_installed_skill() -> SkillInstall | None:
    58→    """Find installed skill document metadata, or None."""
    59→    project_root = get_project_root()
    60→    for rel_path in SKILL_SEARCH_PATHS:
    61→        full = project_root / rel_path
    62→        if not full.is_file():
    63→            continue
    64→        try:
    65→            content = full.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
    66→        except OSError as exc:
    67→            _ = exc
    68→            continue
    69→        version_match = SKILL_VERSION_RE.search(content)
    70→        if not version_match:
    71→            continue
    72→        installed_version = int(version_match.group(1))
    73→        overlay_match = SKILL_OVERLAY_RE.search(content)
    74→        overlay = overlay_match.group(1) if overlay_match else None
    75→        return SkillInstall(
    76→            rel_path=rel_path,
    77→            version=installed_version,
    78→            overlay=overlay,
    79→            stale=installed_version < SKILL_VERSION,
    80→        )
    81→    return None
    82→
    83→
    84→def check_skill_version() -> str | None:
    85→    """Return a warning if installed skill doc is outdated."""
    86→    install = find_installed_skill()
    87→    if not install or not install.stale:
    88→        return None
    89→    return (
    90→        f"Your desloppify skill document is outdated "
    91→        f"(v{install.version}, current v{SKILL_VERSION}). "
    92→        "Run: desloppify update-skill"
    93→    )
    94→
    95→
    96→__all__ = [
    97→    "SKILL_VERSION",
    98→    "SKILL_VERSION_RE",
    99→    "SKILL_OVERLAY_RE",
   100→    "SKILL_BEGIN",
   101→    "SKILL_END",
   102→    "SKILL_SEARCH_PATHS",
   103→    "SKILL_TARGETS",
   104→    "SkillInstall",
   105→    "find_installed_skill",
   106→    "check_skill_version",
   107→]
   108→
```

1→## Hermes Agent Overlay
     2→
     3→Hermes Agent supports parallel execution via worktree isolation (`hermes -w`).
     4→Use separate worktree sessions for parallel review and triage work.
     5→
     6→### Review workflow
     7→
     8→1. Run `desloppify review --prepare` to generate `query.json` and `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json`.
     9→2. Split dimensions into 3-4 batches by theme (e.g., naming + clarity,
    10→   abstraction + error consistency, testing + coverage).
    11→3. Launch parallel Hermes sessions with worktree isolation, one per batch:
    12→   ```
    13→   hermes -w -q "Score these dimensions: <list>. Read .desloppify/review_packet_blind.json for the blind packet. Score from code evidence only."
    14→   ```
    15→4. Each session writes output to a separate file. Merge assessments
    16→   (average overlapping dimension scores) and concatenate findings.
    17→5. Import: `desloppify review --import merged.json --manual-override --attest "Hermes agents ran blind reviews against review_packet_blind.json" --scan-after-import`.
    18→
    19→Each session must consume `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` (not full
    20→`query.json`) to avoid score anchoring.
    21→
    22→### Triage workflow
    23→
    24→Orchestrate triage with per-stage Hermes sessions:
    25→
    26→1. For each stage (observe → reflect → organize → enrich → sense-check):
    27→   - Get prompt: `desloppify plan triage --stage-prompt <stage>`
    28→   - Launch a Hermes session with that prompt: `hermes -w -q "<prompt>"`
    29→   - Verify: `desloppify plan triage` (check dashboard)
    30→   - Confirm: `desloppify plan triage --confirm <stage> --attestation "..."`
    31→2. Complete: `desloppify plan triage --complete --strategy "..." --attestation "..."`
    32→
    33→Run stages sequentially. Within observe and sense-check, use parallel
    34→worktree sessions (`hermes -w`) for per-dimension-group and per-cluster
    35→batches respectively.
    36→
    37→<!-- desloppify-overlay: hermes -->
    38→<!-- desloppify-end -->
    39→
```
