1→"""Project-wide + language-specific config (.desloppify/config.json)."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import copy
     6→import json
     7→import logging
     8→import shutil
     9→import sys
    10→from pathlib import Path
    11→from typing import Any
    12→
    13→from .migration import (
    14→    _migrate_from_state_files as _migrate_from_state_files_impl,
    15→)
    16→from .schema import (
    17→    CONFIG_SCHEMA,
    18→    DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE,
    19→    MAX_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE,
    20→    MIN_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE,
    21→    _coerce_target_strict_score,
    22→    coerce_target_score,
    23→    default_config,
    24→    target_strict_score_from_config,
    25→)
    26→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import safe_write_text
    27→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    28→from desloppify.base.output.fallbacks import log_best_effort_failure
    29→
    30→
    31→def _rename_key(d: dict, old: str, new: str) -> bool:
    32→    if old not in d:
    33→        return False
    34→    d.setdefault(new, d.pop(old))
    35→    return True
    36→
    37→
    38→def _default_config_file() -> Path:
    39→    """Resolve config path from the active runtime project root."""
    40→    return get_project_root() / ".desloppify" / "config.json"
    41→
    42→
    43→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    44→
    45→
    46→def _load_config_json(path: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
    47→    payload = json.loads(path.read_text())
    48→    if not isinstance(payload, dict):
    49→        raise ValueError("config file root must be a JSON object")
    50→    return payload
    51→
    52→
    53→def _load_config_payload(path: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
    54→    if path.exists():
    55→        try:
    56→            return _load_config_json(path)
    57→        except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError, ValueError) as ex:
    58→            backup = path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
    59→            if backup.exists():
    60→                logger.warning(
    61→                    "Primary config load failed for %s; attempting backup %s: %s",
    62→                    path,
    63→                    backup,
    64→                    ex,
    65→                )
    66→                try:
    67→                    backup_payload = _load_config_json(backup)
    68→                    logger.warning(
    69→                        "Recovered config from backup %s after primary load failure at %s",
    70→                        backup,
    71→                        path,
    72→                    )
    73→                    print(
    74→                        f"  ⚠ Config file corrupted ({ex}), loaded from backup.",
    75→                        file=sys.stderr,
    76→                    )
    77→                    return backup_payload
    78→                except (
    79→                    json.JSONDecodeError,
    80→                    UnicodeDecodeError,
    81→                    OSError,
    82→                    ValueError,
    83→                ) as backup_ex:
    84→                    logger.warning(
    85→                        "Backup config load failed from %s after corruption in %s: %s",
    86→                        backup,
    87→                        path,
    88→                        backup_ex,
    89→                    )
    90→                    logger.debug(
    91→                        "Backup config load failed from %s: %s",
    92→                        backup,
    93→                        backup_ex,
    94→                    )
    95→            logger.warning(
    96→                "Config file load failed for %s and backup recovery was unavailable. "
    97→                "Falling back to defaults: %s",
    98→                path,
    99→                ex,
   100→            )
   101→            print(f"  ⚠ Config file corrupted ({ex}). Using defaults.", file=sys.stderr)
   102→            rename_failed = False
   103→            try:
   104→                path.rename(path.with_suffix(".json.corrupted"))
   105→            except OSError as rename_ex:
   106→                rename_failed = True
   107→                logger.debug(
   108→                    "Failed to rename corrupted config file %s: %s",
   109→                    path,
   110→                    rename_ex,
   111→                )
   112→            if rename_failed:
   113→                logger.debug(
   114→                    "Corrupted config file retained at original path: %s",
   115→                    path,
   116→                )
   117→            return {}
   118→    # First run — try migrating from state files
   119→    return _migrate_from_state_files(path)
   120→
   121→
   122→def _migrate_legacy_noise_keys(config: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
   123→    changed = False
   124→    for old, new in (
   125→        ("finding_noise_budget", "issue_noise_budget"),
   126→        ("finding_noise_global_budget", "issue_noise_global_budget"),
   127→    ):
   128→        changed |= _rename_key(config, old, new)
   129→    return changed
   130→
   131→
   132→def _apply_schema_defaults_and_normalization(config: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
   133→    changed = False
   134→    for key, schema in CONFIG_SCHEMA.items():
   135→        if key not in config:
   136→            config[key] = copy.deepcopy(schema.default)
   137→            changed = True
   138→            continue
   139→        if key != "badge_path":
   140→            continue
   141→        try:
   142→            normalized = _validate_badge_path(str(config[key]))
   143→            if normalized != config[key]:
   144→                config[key] = normalized
   145→                changed = True
   146→        except ValueError:
   147→            config[key] = copy.deepcopy(schema.default)
   148→            changed = True
   149→    return changed
   150→
   151→
   152→def load_config(path: Path | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
   153→    """Load config from disk, auto-migrating from state files if needed.
   154→
   155→    Fills missing keys with defaults. If no config.json exists, attempts
   156→    migration from state-*.json files.
   157→    """
   158→    p = path or _default_config_file()
   159→    config = _load_config_payload(p)
   160→    changed = _migrate_legacy_noise_keys(config)
   161→    changed |= _apply_schema_defaults_and_normalization(config)
   162→
   163→    if changed and p.exists():
   164→        try:
   165→            save_config(config, p)
   166→        except OSError as exc:
   167→            log_best_effort_failure(logger, f"persist migrated config to {p}", exc)
   168→
   169→    return config
   170→
   171→
   172→def save_config(config: dict, path: Path | None = None) -> None:
   173→    """Save config to disk atomically."""
   174→    p = path or _default_config_file()
   175→    if p.exists():
   176→        backup = p.with_suffix(".json.bak")
   177→        try:
   178→            shutil.copy2(str(p), str(backup))
   179→        except OSError as backup_ex:
   180→            logger.debug(
   181→                "Failed to create config backup %s: %s",
   182→                backup,
   183→                backup_ex,
   184→            )
   185→    safe_write_text(p, json.dumps(config, indent=2) + "\n")
   186→
   187→
   188→def add_ignore_pattern(config: dict, pattern: str) -> None:
   189→    """Append a pattern to the ignore list (deduplicates)."""
   190→    ignores = config.setdefault("ignore", [])
   191→    if pattern not in ignores:
   192→        ignores.append(pattern)
   193→
   194→
   195→def add_exclude_pattern(config: dict, pattern: str) -> None:
   196→    """Append a pattern to the exclude list (deduplicates)."""
   197→    excludes = config.setdefault("exclude", [])
   198→    if pattern not in excludes:
   199→        excludes.append(pattern)
   200→
   201→
   202→def set_ignore_metadata(config: dict, pattern: str, *, note: str, added_at: str) -> None:
   203→    """Record note + timestamp for an ignore pattern."""
   204→    meta = config.setdefault("ignore_metadata", {})
   205→    if not isinstance(meta, dict):
   206→        meta = {}
   207→        config["ignore_metadata"] = meta
   208→    meta[pattern] = {"note": note, "added_at": added_at}
   209→
   210→
   211→def _validate_badge_path(raw: str) -> str:
   212→    """Require badge_path to point to a filename (root or nested path)."""
   213→    value = raw.strip()
   214→    path = Path(value)
   215→    if (
   216→        not value
   217→        or value.endswith(("/", "\\"))
   218→        or path.name in {"", ".", ".."}
   219→    ):
   220→        raise ValueError(
   221→            "Expected file path for badge_path "
   222→            f"(example: scorecard.png or assets/scorecard.png), got: {raw}"
   223→        )
   224→    return value
   225→
   226→
   227→def _set_int_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   228→    if raw.lower() == "never":
   229→        config[key] = 0
   230→    else:
   231→        config[key] = int(raw)
   232→    if key != "target_strict_score":
   233→        return
   234→    target_strict_score, target_valid = _coerce_target_strict_score(config[key])
   235→    if not target_valid:
   236→        raise ValueError(
   237→            f"Expected integer {MIN_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE}-{MAX_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE} "
   238→            f"for {key}, got: {raw}"
   239→        )
   240→    config[key] = target_strict_score
   241→
   242→
   243→def _set_bool_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   244→    normalized = raw.lower()
   245→    if normalized in ("true", "1", "yes"):
   246→        config[key] = True
   247→        return
   248→    if normalized in ("false", "0", "no"):
   249→        config[key] = False
   250→        return
   251→    raise ValueError(f"Expected true/false for {key}, got: {raw}")
   252→
   253→
   254→def _set_str_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   255→    if key == "badge_path":
   256→        config[key] = _validate_badge_path(raw)
   257→        return
   258→    config[key] = raw
   259→
   260→
   261→def _set_list_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   262→    config.setdefault(key, [])
   263→    if raw not in config[key]:
   264→        config[key].append(raw)
   265→
   266→
   267→_SCHEMA_SETTERS = {
   268→    int: _set_int_config_value,
   269→    bool: _set_bool_config_value,
   270→    str: _set_str_config_value,
   271→    list: _set_list_config_value,
   272→}
   273→
   274→
   275→def set_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   276→    """Parse and set a config value from a raw string.
   277→
   278→    Handles special cases:
   279→    - "never" → 0 for age keys
   280→    - "true"/"false" for bools
   281→    """
   282→    if key not in CONFIG_SCHEMA:
   283→        raise KeyError(f"Unknown config key: {key}")
   284→
   285→    schema = CONFIG_SCHEMA[key]
   286→    setter = _SCHEMA_SETTERS.get(schema.type)
   287→    if setter is not None:
   288→        setter(config, key, raw)
   289→        return
   290→    if schema.type is dict:
   291→        raise ValueError(f"Cannot set dict key '{key}' via CLI — use subcommands")
   292→    config[key] = raw
   293→
   294→
   295→def unset_config_value(config: dict, key: str) -> None:
   296→    """Reset a config key to its default value."""
   297→    if key not in CONFIG_SCHEMA:
   298→        raise KeyError(f"Unknown config key: {key}")
   299→    config[key] = copy.deepcopy(CONFIG_SCHEMA[key].default)
   300→
   301→
   302→def config_for_query(config: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
   303→    """Return a sanitized config dict suitable for query.json."""
   304→    return {k: config.get(k, schema.default) for k, schema in CONFIG_SCHEMA.items()}
   305→
   306→
   307→def _migrate_from_state_files(config_path: Path) -> dict:
   308→    """Compatibility wrapper for state-file config migration."""
   309→    return _migrate_from_state_files_impl(config_path, save_config_fn=save_config)
   310→
   311→
   312→__all__ = [
   313→    "CONFIG_SCHEMA",
   314→    "DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE",
   315→    "MAX_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE",
   316→    "MIN_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE",
   317→    "add_exclude_pattern",
   318→    "add_ignore_pattern",
   319→    "coerce_target_score",
   320→    "config_for_query",

   325→    "set_ignore_metadata",
   326→    "target_strict_score_from_config",
   327→    "unset_config_value",
   328→]

   111→def default_config() -> dict[str, Any]:
   112→    """Return a config dict with all keys set to their defaults."""
   113→    return {k: copy.deepcopy(v.default) for k, v in CONFIG_SCHEMA.items()}
   114→
   115→
   116→def _coerce_target_strict_score(value: object) -> tuple[int, bool]:
   117→    """Coerce target strict score and report whether it is in range."""
   118→    try:
   119→        parsed = int(value)
   120→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   121→        return MIN_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE, False
   122→    valid = MIN_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE <= parsed <= MAX_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE
   123→    return parsed, valid
   124→
   125→
   126→def coerce_target_score(
   127→    value: object, *, fallback: float = DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE
   128→) -> float:
   129→    """Normalize target score-like values to a safe [0, 100] float."""
   130→    if is_numeric(fallback):
   131→        fallback_value = float(fallback)
   132→    else:
   133→        fallback_value = DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE
   134→
   135→    if is_numeric(value):
   136→        parsed = float(value)
   137→    elif isinstance(value, str):
   138→        text = value.strip()
   139→        if not text:
   140→            parsed = fallback_value
   141→        else:
   142→            try:
   143→                parsed = float(text)
   144→            except ValueError:
   145→                parsed = fallback_value
   146→    else:
   147→        parsed = fallback_value
   148→    return max(0.0, min(100.0, parsed))
   149→
   150→
   151→def target_strict_score_from_config(
   152→    config: dict | None, *, fallback: float = DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE
   153→) -> float:
   154→    """Read and normalize target strict score from config."""
   155→    if isinstance(config, dict):
   156→        raw = config.get("target_strict_score", fallback)
   157→    else:
   158→        raw = fallback
   159→    return coerce_target_score(raw, fallback=fallback)
   160→
   161→

   163→    "CONFIG_SCHEMA",
   164→    "DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE",

1→"""Lightweight integrity helpers for subjective/review scoring.
     2→
     3→This module intentionally lives outside ``desloppify.intelligence.review`` so command/state
     4→paths can import it without loading the heavier review package.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from collections.abc import Iterable, Mapping
    10→
    11→from desloppify.engine._state.issue_semantics import is_assessment_request
    12→from desloppify.engine._scoring.policy.core import (
    13→    SUBJECTIVE_TARGET_MATCH_TOLERANCE,
    14→    matches_target_score,
    15→)
    16→
    17→__all__ = [
    18→    "SUBJECTIVE_TARGET_MATCH_TOLERANCE",
    19→    "is_holistic_subjective_issue",
    20→    "is_subjective_review_open",
    21→    "matches_target_score",
    22→    "subjective_review_open_breakdown",
    23→    "unassessed_subjective_dimensions",
    24→]
    25→
    26→
    27→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    28→# Internal iteration helper
    29→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    30→
    31→
    32→def _iter_issues(
    33→    issues: Mapping[str, dict] | Iterable[dict],
    34→) -> Iterable[tuple[str, dict]]:
    35→    """Yield (issue_id, issue) pairs from mapping or iterable inputs."""
    36→    if isinstance(issues, Mapping):
    37→        for issue_id, issue in issues.items():
    38→            if isinstance(issue, dict):
    39→                yield str(issue_id), issue
    40→        return
    41→
    42→    for index, issue in enumerate(issues):
    43→        if isinstance(issue, dict):
    44→            yield str(index), issue
    45→
    46→
    47→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    48→# Public helpers (formerly in integrity/review.py)
    49→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    50→
    51→
    52→def is_subjective_review_open(issue: dict) -> bool:
    53→    """Return True when a issue is an open subjective-review signal."""
    54→    return issue.get("status") == "open" and is_assessment_request(issue)
    55→
    56→
    57→def is_holistic_subjective_issue(issue: dict, *, issue_id: str = "") -> bool:
    58→    """Best-effort check for holistic subjective-review coverage issues.
    59→
    60→    With dimension-level subjective_review issues, this checks for legacy
    61→    holistic markers (backward compat) and the new dimension-level format.
    62→    """
    63→    candidate_id = str(issue.get("id") or issue_id or "")
    64→    # Legacy holistic markers
    65→    if "::holistic_unreviewed" in candidate_id or "::holistic_stale" in candidate_id:
    66→        return True
    67→
    68→    summary = str(issue.get("summary", "") or "").lower()
    69→    if "holistic" in summary and "review" in summary:
    70→        return True
    71→
    72→    detail = issue.get("detail", {})
    73→    if detail.get("holistic"):
    74→        return True
    75→
    76→    # Dimension-level issues are codebase-wide by nature
    77→    if is_assessment_request(issue) and detail.get("dimension"):
    78→        return True
    79→
    80→    return False
    81→
    82→
    83→def subjective_review_open_breakdown(
    84→    issues: Mapping[str, dict] | Iterable[dict],
    85→) -> tuple[int, dict[str, int], dict[str, int]]:
    86→    """Return open subjective count plus reason and dimension breakdowns."""
    87→    reason_counts: dict[str, int] = {}
    88→    dimension_counts: dict[str, int] = {}
    89→    total = 0
    90→
    91→    for issue_id, issue in _iter_issues(issues):
    92→        if not is_subjective_review_open(issue):
    93→            continue
    94→
    95→        total += 1
    96→        detail = issue.get("detail", {})
    97→        reason = str(detail.get("reason", "other") or "other")
    98→        reason_counts[reason] = reason_counts.get(reason, 0) + 1
    99→
   100→        dim = str(detail.get("dimension", "") or "")
   101→        if dim:
   102→            dimension_counts[dim] = dimension_counts.get(dim, 0) + 1
   103→
   104→    return total, reason_counts, dimension_counts
   105→
   106→
   107→def unassessed_subjective_dimensions(dim_scores: dict | None) -> list[str]:
   108→    """Return subjective dimension display names that are still 0% placeholders."""
   109→    if not dim_scores:
   110→        return []
   111→
   112→    unassessed: list[str] = []
   113→    for name, info in dim_scores.items():
   114→        detectors = info.get("detectors", {})
   115→        if "subjective_assessment" not in detectors:
   116→            continue
   117→        assessment_meta = detectors.get("subjective_assessment", {})
   118→        if isinstance(assessment_meta, dict) and assessment_meta.get("placeholder"):
   119→            unassessed.append(name)
   120→            continue
   121→        strict_val = float(info.get("strict", info.get("score", 100.0)))
   122→        issues = int(info.get("failing", 0))
   123→        if strict_val <= 0.0 and issues == 0:
   124→            unassessed.append(name)
   125→
   126→    unassessed.sort()
   127→    return unassessed
   128→

"""Unified work-queue entrypoints for next/show/plan views."""

from __future__ import annotations

from desloppify.engine._work_queue.context import QueueContext
from desloppify.engine._work_queue.finalize import finalize_queue
from desloppify.engine._work_queue.helpers import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
from desloppify.engine._work_queue.inputs import resolve_queue_inputs
from desloppify.engine._work_queue.models import (
    QueueBuildOptions,
    QueueVisibility,
    WorkQueueResult,
)
from desloppify.engine._work_queue.plan_order import collapse_clusters
from desloppify.engine._work_queue.ranking import group_queue_items
from desloppify.engine._work_queue.selection import select_queue_items
from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel

def build_work_queue(
    state: StateModel,
    *,
    options: QueueBuildOptions | None = None,
) -> WorkQueueResult:
    """Build the active canonical work queue for the current invocation."""
    return _build_work_queue_with_visibility(
        state,
        options=options,
        visibility=QueueVisibility.ALL,
    )


def _build_work_queue_with_visibility(
    state: StateModel,
    *,
    options: QueueBuildOptions | None = None,
    visibility: str = QueueVisibility.ALL,
) -> WorkQueueResult:
    """Build a ranked work queue from canonical queue partitions.

    Open-status queue views are resolved from the canonical queue snapshot so
    lifecycle phase, execution visibility, and backlog visibility all come
    from one source of truth. Non-open status views remain issue-list queries.
    """
    opts = options or QueueBuildOptions()
    plan, scan_path, status, threshold = resolve_queue_inputs(opts, state)
    items = select_queue_items(
        state,
        opts=opts,
        plan=plan,
        scan_path=scan_path,
        status=status,
        threshold=threshold,
        visibility=visibility,
    )
    return finalize_queue(items, state=state, plan=plan, opts=opts)

__all__ = [
    "ATTEST_EXAMPLE",
    "QueueBuildOptions",
    "QueueContext",
    "QueueVisibility",
    "WorkQueueResult",
    "_build_work_queue_with_visibility",
    "build_work_queue",
    "collapse_clusters",
    "group_queue_items",
]

"""Resolve command handlers."""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse

import desloppify.intelligence.narrative.core as narrative_mod
from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.attestation import (
    show_note_length_requirement,
    validate_note_length,
)
from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.guardrails import require_triage_current_or_exit
from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import resolve_lang
from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state_persistence import save_state_or_exit
from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.queue_progress import show_score_with_plan_context
from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
from desloppify.engine._state.resolution import coerce_assessment_score
from desloppify.state_io import load_state

from .apply import _resolve_all_patterns, _write_resolve_query_entry
from .living_plan import update_living_plan_after_resolve
from .messages import print_fixed_next_user_message, print_no_match_warning
from .plan_load import load_resolve_plan_access
from .queue_guard import _check_queue_order_guard
from .render import (
    _print_next_command,
    _print_resolve_summary,
    _print_subjective_reset_hint,
    _print_wontfix_batch_warning,
    render_commit_guidance,
)
from .selection import (
    ResolveQueryContext,
    _enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation,
    _previous_score_snapshot,
    _validate_resolve_inputs,
)


def _validate_fixed_note(args: argparse.Namespace) -> bool:
    if args.status != "fixed":
        return True
    note = getattr(args, "note", None)
    if validate_note_length(note):
        return True
    show_note_length_requirement(note)
    return False


def _load_state_with_guards(
    args: argparse.Namespace,
    *,
    attestation: str | None,
) -> tuple[str, dict, object] | None:
    """Validate inputs, apply guardrails, and load state for resolve."""
    _validate_resolve_inputs(args, attestation)
    if not _validate_fixed_note(args):
        return None

    state_file = state_path(args)
    state = load_state(state_file)
    plan_access = load_resolve_plan_access()

    if not getattr(args, "force_resolve", False):
        if _check_queue_order_guard(
            state,
            args.patterns,
            args.status,
            plan_access=plan_access,
        ):
            return None

    if args.status == "fixed":
        require_triage_current_or_exit(
            state=state,
            plan=plan_access.plan if isinstance(plan_access.plan, dict) and not plan_access.degraded else None,
            patterns=args.patterns,
            bypass=bool(getattr(args, "force_resolve", False)),
            attest=getattr(args, "attest", "") or "",
        )

    return state_file, state, plan_access


def _resolve_ids_with_snapshots(
    state: dict,
    args: argparse.Namespace,
    *,
    attestation: str | None,
    plan_access,
) -> tuple[object, dict[str, float], list[str]]:
    """Apply resolve patterns and return pre/post context for rendering."""
    _enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation(
        state,
        args,
        attestation=attestation,
        resolve_all_patterns_fn=_resolve_all_patterns,
    )

def cmd_plan_output(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    """Generate a prioritized markdown plan from state."""
    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    state = runtime.state

    if not require_issue_inventory(state):
        return

    config_warning = check_config_staleness(runtime.config)
    if config_warning:
        print(colorize(f"  {config_warning}", "yellow"))

    plan_md = planning_mod.generate_plan_md(state)
    next_command = "desloppify next --count 20"

    output = getattr(args, "output", None)
    if output:
        try:
            from pathlib import Path
            output_path = Path(output)
            if output_path.exists():
                print(colorize(
                    f"  {output} already exists — refusing to overwrite.",
                    "red",
                ))
                print(colorize(
                    f"  Delete it first (`desloppify plan reset` or `rm {output}`), "
                    f"or choose a different filename.",
                    "dim",
                ))
                return
            safe_write_text(output, plan_md)
            print(colorize(f"Plan written to {output}", "green"))
            print_agent_plan(
                ["Inspect and execute the generated plan."],
                next_command=next_command,
            )
        except OSError as e:
            warn_best_effort(f"Could not write plan to {output}: {e}")
    else:
        print(plan_md)
        print()
        print_agent_plan(
            ["Start from the top-ranked action in this plan."],

1→# Desloppify — Technical Internals
     2→
     3→Traditional tools catch mechanical issues — linters, formatters, dead code finders. Desloppify wraps those but the point is **subjective analysis**: structured LLM prompts about architecture, design quality, and convention consistency, tracked as scored findings. The score weights subjective findings heavily because that's what actually moves the needle. See the top-level README for philosophy and usage.
     4→
     5→## Directory Layout
     6→
     7→```
     8→desloppify/
     9→├── cli.py              # Argparse, main()
    10→├── state.py            # Compatibility facade (prefer state_io/state_scoring)
    11→├── app/                # CLI layer (commands, parser, output)
    12→├── base/               # Foundational shared infrastructure
    13→│   ├── config.py       # Configuration loading and helpers
    14→│   ├── coercions.py    # Type coercion utilities
    15→│   ├── enums.py        # Tier, Confidence, Status, Zone enums
    16→│   ├── exception_sets.py # CommandError and exception groups
    17→│   ├── registry.py     # Canonical detector registry
    18→│   ├── scoring_constants.py # CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS, HOLISTIC_MULTIPLIER
    19→│   ├── text_utils.py   # Project root, path helpers
    20→│   ├── discovery/      # File discovery, path resolution
    21→│   ├── output/         # Terminal output, colorize, issues rendering
    22→│   ├── search/         # Grep, query engine
    23→│   └── text/           # Text API, rel(), is_numeric()
    24→├── engine/             # Scan/scoring/state internals
    25→│   ├── detectors/      # Generic algorithms (zero language knowledge)
    26→│   ├── hook_registry.py # Detector-safe language hook registry
    27→│   ├── plan.py         # Compatibility shim over plan facades
    28→│   ├── plan_state.py   # Public plan state/schema/policy facade
    29→│   ├── plan_ops.py     # Public plan mutation facade
    30→│   ├── _plan/         # Plan persistence, sync, lifecycle internals
    31→│   ├── _work_queue/   # Canonical queue snapshot and queue assembly
    32→│   ├── plan_triage.py  # Public triage commands/prompts facade
    33→│   ├── planning/       # Prioritization and plan generation
    34→│   ├── policy/         # Zones, scoring policy
    35→│   ├── _scoring/
    36→│   ├── _state/
    37→│   └── _work_queue/
    38→├── intelligence/       # Subjective/narrative/review layer
    39→│   ├── narrative/
    40→│   ├── integrity/
    41→│   └── review/
    42→└── languages/          # Language plugins (auto-discovered, see languages/README.md)
    43→    ├── _framework/     # Shared plugin framework, generic_lang(), tree-sitter
    44→    ├── python/         # Full plugins (custom detectors, fixers, review dims)
    45→    ├── typescript/
    46→    ├── csharp/, dart/, gdscript/, go/, rust/
    47→    └── ruby/, java/, ... (21 generic plugins)
    48→```
    49→
    50→## Architecture
    51→
    52→```
    53→Layer 0: base/                   Foundational infrastructure. Path resolution, config, enums, output.
    54→Layer 1: engine/detectors/       Generic algorithms. Data-in, data-out. Zero language imports.
    55→Layer 2: languages/_framework/   Shared contracts/helpers. Normalize raw results → tiered findings.
    56→Layer 3: languages/<name>/       Language config + phases + extractors + detectors + fixers.
    57→Layer 4: app/                    CLI commands. Thin entry points delegating to engine/intelligence.
    58→```
    59→
    60→**Import direction**: Each layer imports only from lower-numbered layers. `languages/` → `engine/detectors/`. Never the reverse. Detectors needing language-specific behavior use `engine.hook_registry.get_lang_hook(...)`.
    61→
    62→## Domain Glossary
    63→
    64→- **Finding**: A detected issue (`TypedDict` in `state.py`). Has detector, tier, category, file, description.
    65→- **Detector**: A named analysis algorithm. Registry in `base/registry.py`.
    66→- **Tier**: Severity T1–T4 (T4 = architectural, highest weight). Enum in `base/enums.py`.
    67→- **Zone**: File intent classification — production/test/config/generated/script/vendor. Deterministic path-based.
    68→- **Dimension**: A subjective quality axis (naming, structure, patterns, etc.) scored by LLM review.
    69→- **Phase**: A scan stage — structural, style, security, etc. Each phase runs extractors → detectors → normalization.
    70→- **Narrative**: Coaching layer that computes phase/headline/actions/reminders from scan state.
    71→- **Concern**: A logical grouping of related findings (e.g. "unused code in auth module").
    72→- **Plan**: Prioritized work queue in `.desloppify/plan.json` — ordered, clustered, with overrides.
    73→
    74→## Data Flow
    75→
    76→```
    77→scan:    LangConfig → LangRun(phases) → generate_findings() → merge_scan() → state-{lang}.json
    78→plan:    state → reconcile → plan.json (ordered queue, clusters, deferred items)
    79→review:  state + plan → batch packets → LLM → import findings → merge
    80→fix:     LangConfig.fixers → fixer.fix() → resolve in state
    81→next:    state + plan → highest-priority item with coaching
    82→detect:  LangConfig.detect_commands[name](args) → display
    83→```
    84→
    85→## Contracts
    86→
    87→**Detector**: `detect_*(data, config) → list[dict]` — generic algorithm, no language assumptions.
    88→
    89→**Phase runner**: `_phase_*(path, lang) → (list[Finding], dict[str, int])` — thin orchestrator calling extractors → generic algorithms → normalization.
    90→
    91→**LangConfig**: Static language contract. Owns phases, detectors, thresholds, hooks.
    92→
    93→**LangRun**: Per-invocation runtime wrapper (`_framework/runtime.py`) carrying mutable state (zone_map, dep_graph, complexity_map). Phases execute against LangRun, not LangConfig.
    94→
    95→## Rules
    96→
    97→- Entry command modules stay thin — behavioral logic in delegated modules
    98→- Prefer narrow plan surfaces (`engine.plan_state`, `engine.plan_ops`, `engine.plan_triage`, `engine.planning`) over `engine.plan` in new code
    99→- Dynamic imports only in `languages/__init__.py` (discovery) and `engine/hook_registry.py` (hooks)
   100→- Persistent schema is owned by `engine/_state/`; `state.py` is compatibility-only. Command modules don't introduce ad-hoc persisted fields
   101→- `LangRun` owns per-run mutable state, not `LangConfig`
   102→- `base/` has zero upward imports — it never imports from `engine/`, `app/`, `intelligence/`, or `languages/`
   103→
   104→## Where Do I Put This?
   105→
   106→- **New detector**: `engine/detectors/` — pure algorithm, no language imports
   107→- **New command**: `app/commands/` — thin entry point, delegate logic to engine/intelligence
   108→- **New language plugin**: `languages/<name>/` — implement `LangConfig`, add phases
   109→- **Path resolution / file helpers**: `base/discovery/`
   110→- **Scoring constants**: `base/scoring_constants.py`
   111→- **Configuration helpers**: `base/config.py`
   112→- **New enum / shared type**: `base/enums.py`
   113→
   114→## Non-Obvious Behavior
   115→
   116→- **State scoping**: `merge_scan` only auto-resolves findings matching the scan's `lang` and `scan_path`. A Python scan never touches TS state.
   117→- **Suspect guard**: If a detector drops from >=5 findings to 0, disappearances are held (bypass: `--force-resolve`).
   118→- **Scoring**: Weighted by tier (T4=4x, T1=1x). Strict score penalizes both open and wontfix.
   119→- **Cascade effects**: Fixing one category (e.g. unused imports) can surface work for the next (unused vars). Score can temporarily drop.
   120→- **Tree-sitter optional**: All tree-sitter features degrade gracefully. Without `tree-sitter-language-pack`, generic plugins fall back to tool-only mode.
   121→- **Bandit optional for Python depth**: Without `bandit`, Python-specific security checks are skipped; scan surfaces preflight/post-scan coverage warnings and marks score confidence reduced for security.
   122→
```

"""CLI entrypoint for review command."""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass

from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import resolve_lang
from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.command_runtime import command_runtime
from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_scan_metrics
from desloppify.base.exception_sets import CommandError

from .batch.orchestrator import do_import_run, do_run_batches
from .external import do_external_start, do_external_submit
from .importing.cmd import do_import, do_validate_import
from .importing.flags import ReviewImportConfig
from .merge import do_merge
from .preflight import review_rerun_preflight
from .prepare import do_prepare


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ReviewOptions:
    """All user-facing review command options extracted once from argparse."""

    merge: bool = False
    run_batches: bool = False
    import_run_dir: str | None = None
    external_start: bool = False
    external_submit: bool = False
    import_file: str | None = None
    validate_import_file: str | None = None
    session_id: str | None = None
    allow_partial: bool = False
    scan_after_import: bool = False
    path: str = "."
    dry_run: bool = False
    manual_override: bool = False
    attested_external: bool = False
    attest: str | None = None

    @classmethod
    def from_args(cls, args: argparse.Namespace) -> ReviewOptions:
        return cls(
            merge=bool(getattr(args, "merge", False)),
            run_batches=bool(getattr(args, "run_batches", False)),
            import_run_dir=getattr(args, "import_run_dir", None),
            external_start=bool(getattr(args, "external_start", False)),
            external_submit=bool(getattr(args, "external_submit", False)),
            import_file=getattr(args, "import_file", None),
            validate_import_file=getattr(args, "validate_import_file", None),
            session_id=getattr(args, "session_id", None),
            allow_partial=bool(getattr(args, "allow_partial", False)),
            scan_after_import=bool(getattr(args, "scan_after_import", False)),
            path=str(getattr(args, "path", ".") or "."),
            dry_run=bool(getattr(args, "dry_run", False)),
            manual_override=bool(getattr(args, "manual_override", False)),
            attested_external=bool(getattr(args, "attested_external", False)),
            attest=getattr(args, "attest", None),
        )


def _enable_live_review_output() -> None:
    """Best-effort: force line-buffered review output for non-TTY runners."""
    for stream_name in ("stdout", "stderr"):
        stream = getattr(sys, stream_name, None)
        reconfigure = getattr(stream, "reconfigure", None)
        if not callable(reconfigure):
            continue
        try:
            reconfigure(line_buffering=True, write_through=True)
        except (OSError, ValueError, TypeError) as exc:
            _ = exc


def _require_lang(lang) -> None:
    if lang:
        return
    raise CommandError("Error: could not detect language. Use --lang.", exit_code=1)


def _mode_flags(opts: ReviewOptions) -> list[bool]:
    import_mode = bool(opts.import_file) and not opts.external_submit
    return [
        opts.merge,
        opts.run_batches,
        bool(opts.import_run_dir),
        opts.external_start,
        opts.external_submit,
        import_mode,
        bool(opts.validate_import_file),
    ]


def _validate_mode_selection(
    opts: ReviewOptions,
    *,
    mode_flags: list[bool],
) -> None:
    if sum(1 for enabled in mode_flags if enabled) > 1:
        raise CommandError(
            "Error: choose one review mode per command "
            "(--merge | --run-batches | --import-run | --external-start | --external-submit | --import | --validate-import).",
            exit_code=1,
        )

    if opts.external_submit and not opts.import_file:
        raise CommandError(
            "Error: --external-submit requires --import FILE.",
            exit_code=2,
        )
    if opts.external_submit and not opts.session_id:
        raise CommandError(
            "Error: --external-submit requires --session-id.",
            exit_code=2,
        )


def _is_default_prepare_mode(opts: ReviewOptions, mode_flags: list[bool]) -> bool:
    """True when the command will run the default prepare flow (no explicit mode)."""
    return not any(mode_flags) and not opts.import_file


def _run_review_mode(
    args: argparse.Namespace,
    *,
    opts: ReviewOptions,
    runtime,
    state,
    lang,
    state_file,
) -> None:
    if opts.merge:
        do_merge(args)
        return
    if opts.run_batches:
        review_rerun_preflight(state, args, state_file=state_file)
        do_run_batches(
            args,
            state,
            lang,
            state_file,
            config=runtime.config,
        )
        return
    if opts.import_run_dir:
        do_import_run(
            opts.import_run_dir,
            state,
            lang,
            state_file,
            config=runtime.config,
            allow_partial=opts.allow_partial,
            scan_after_import=opts.scan_after_import,
            scan_path=opts.path,
            dry_run=opts.dry_run,
        )
        return
    if opts.external_start:
        review_rerun_preflight(state, args, state_file=state_file)
        do_external_start(
            args,
            state,
            lang,
            config=runtime.config,
        )
        return
    if opts.external_submit:
        do_external_submit(
            import_file=str(opts.import_file),
            session_id=str(opts.session_id),
            state=state,
            lang=lang,
            state_file=state_file,
            config=runtime.config,
            allow_partial=opts.allow_partial,
            scan_after_import=opts.scan_after_import,
            scan_path=opts.path,
            dry_run=opts.dry_run,
        )
        return
    if opts.validate_import_file:
        do_validate_import(
            opts.validate_import_file,
            lang,
            import_config=ReviewImportConfig(
                allow_partial=opts.allow_partial,
                manual_override=opts.manual_override,
                attested_external=opts.attested_external,
                manual_attest=opts.attest,
            ),
        )
        return

    if opts.import_file:
        do_import(
            opts.import_file,
            state,
            lang,
```

"""directives command: view and manage agent directives."""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse

from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.command_runtime import command_runtime
from desloppify.base.config import save_config
from desloppify.base.exception_sets import CommandError
from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
from desloppify.engine._plan.refresh_lifecycle import VALID_LIFECYCLE_PHASES

# The four phases that actually matter as agent directive hooks.
# Each has: short description, when it fires, example use case.
_PHASES: list[tuple[str, str, str, str]] = [
    (
        "execute",
        "Working the fix queue — code changes, refactors, and fixes.",
        "Fires when the queue fills with work items after triage,\n"
        "    or when you unskip/reopen issues that push back into work mode.",
        "Commit after every 3 fixes. Don't refactor beyond what the issue asks.",
    ),
    (
        "postflight",
        "Execution done — transitioning into planning/review phases.",
        "Fires when the execution queue drains and the system moves into\n"
        "    review, workflow, or triage. Catch-all for leaving work mode.",
        "Stop and summarise what you fixed before continuing.",
    ),
    (
        "triage",
        "Reading issues, deciding what's real, clustering into a plan.",
        "Fires when workflow items complete and triage stages are injected,\n"
        "    or when review surfaces issues that need strategic decisions.",
        "Open every flagged file before deciding. Skip nothing without reading the code.",
    ),
    (
        "review",
        "Scoring subjective dimensions — reading code and assessing quality.",
        "Fires when the system needs subjective scores (first review, stale\n"
        "    dimensions, or post-triage re-review). Covers all review sub-phases.",
        "Use review_packet_blind.json only. Do not read previous scores or targets.",
    ),
    (
        "scan",
        "Running detectors and analyzing the codebase.",
        "Fires when the lifecycle resets to the scan phase after a cycle\n"
        "    completes or when no other phase applies.",
        "Include --skip-slow if this is a mid-cycle rescan.",
    ),
]

_PHASE_NAMES = {name for name, _, _, _ in _PHASES}

_EXAMPLE_DIRECTIVES = {
    "execute": "Commit after every 3 fixes. Don't refactor beyond what the issue asks.",
    "triage": "Open every flagged file before deciding. Skip nothing without reading the code.",
    "review": "Use review_packet_blind.json only. Do not read previous scores or targets.",
}


def cmd_directives(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    """Handle directives subcommands: show, set, unset."""
    action = getattr(args, "directives_action", None)
    if action == "set":
        _directives_set(args)
    elif action == "unset":
        _directives_unset(args)
    else:
        _directives_show(args)


def _directives_show(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    """Show all phases with their directives (if configured)."""
    config = command_runtime(args).config
    messages = config.get("transition_messages", {})
    if not isinstance(messages, dict):
        messages = {}

    active = {
        phase: text
        for phase, text in messages.items()
        if isinstance(text, str) and text.strip()
    }

    print(colorize("\n  Agent Directives\n", "bold"))
    print(
        "  Messages shown to AI agents at lifecycle phase transitions.\n"
        "  Use them to switch models, set constraints, or give context-\n"
        "  specific instructions at key moments in the workflow.\n"
    )

    for name, description, when, example_use in _PHASES:
        directive = active.get(name)
        if directive:
            marker = colorize("*", "green")
            print(f"  {marker} {colorize(name, 'cyan')}  {description}")
            print(colorize(f"    When: {when}", "dim"))
            print(f"    Directive: {directive}")
        else:

## Comprehensive Desloppify Project Exploration

Based on my thorough exploration of `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify`, here's a detailed analysis:

### 1. **What is Desloppify? What does it do?**

Desloppify is a **multi-language codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker** designed to help AI agents (and human developers) improve code quality systematically. It's distinct from traditional linters because it combines:

- **Mechanical detection**: Dead code, duplication, complexity, security issues, unused imports
- **Subjective LLM review**: Named abstractions, module boundaries, naming clarity, error handling patterns
- **Persistent state tracking**: Issues are tracked across scans so the system can show progress
- **Anti-gaming scoring**: The score (0-100) is weighted to resist manipulation—only actual improvements increase it

Key philosophy: Move from "vibe coding" (fast & loose) to "vibe engineering" (thoughtful & beautiful). Target strict score is typically 95+.

### 2. **How is it structured? Key files and modules**

```
desloppify/
├── cli.py                          # Main entry point (argparse + dispatch)
├── state.py / state_io.py          # Persistent state schema & I/O
├── app/                            # CLI commands layer
│   ├── commands/                   # 18+ command implementations
│   │   ├── scan/                   # Run all detectors, update state
│   │   ├── next/                   # Show highest-priority queue item
│   │   ├── review/                 # LLM subjective scoring workflow
│   │   ├── resolve/                # Mark issues fixed/wontfix
│   │   ├── plan/                   # Prioritization, triage, clustering
│   │   ├── backlog/                # Show all open work
│   │   ├── show/                   # Display issues by query
│   │   ├── status/                 # Current state summary
│   │   ├── exclude/                # Manage excluded paths
│   │   ├── directives/             # Agent lifecycle hooks
│   │   ├── move/                   # File refactoring support
│   │   ├── detect/                 # Raw detector invocation
│   │   └── ...9 more commands
│   ├── cli_support/                # Argparse parser groups
│   └── output/                     # Terminal rendering, scorecard
├── base/                           # Foundational infrastructure
│   ├── config/                     # Config schema, loading, defaults
│   ├── registry.py                 # Canonical detector registry
│   ├── discovery/                  # File path resolution
│   ├── output/                     # Terminal colorizing, rendering
│   ├── search/                     # Query engine
│   ├── scoring_constants.py        # Tier weights, confidence weights
│   └── enums.py                    # Tier, Status, Zone, Confidence
├── engine/                         # Core computation layer
│   ├── detectors/                  # Generic algorithms (unaware of language)
│   ├── _state/                     # State schema, persistence, merging
│   ├── _scoring/                   # Score computation, policy
│   ├── _work_queue/                # Queue assembly, ranking, filtering
│   ├── _plan/                      # Plan persistence, lifecycle
│   ├── planning/                   # Prioritization, clustering, ordering
│   ├── policy/                     # Zone classification, scoring policy
│   └── hook_registry.py            # Language-specific hook injection
├── intelligence/                   # Subjective/narrative layer
│   ├── review/                     # Dimension selection, batching, importing
│   ├── narrative/                  # Phase/headline/action generation
│   └── integrity.py                # Lightweight integrity checks
└── languages/                      # 29 language plugins
    ├── _framework/                 # Shared plugin framework
    ├── python/, typescript/, ...   # Full plugins (8 languages)
    └── ruby/, java/, ...           # Generic plugins (21 languages)
```

### 3. **How does it interact with Hermes?**

**Desloppify explicitly supports Hermes Agent orchestration** via a published overlay in `docs/HERMES.md`. Integration points:

**Review Workflow:**
- Run `desloppify review --prepare` → generates blind review packet (`.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json`)
- Spin up parallel Hermes sessions with worktree isolation (`hermes -w -q "..."`), split by dimension themes
- Each session scores subjective dimensions blind (no anchoring bias)
- Merge results and import: `desloppify review --import merged.json --manual-override --attest "Hermes agents ran..."`

**Triage Workflow:**
- Run per-stage (observe → reflect → organize → enrich → sense-check)
- Get prompt: `desloppify plan triage --stage-prompt <stage>`
- Launch Hermes sessions: `hermes -w -q "<prompt>"`
- Verify/confirm stage: `desloppify plan triage --confirm <stage> --attestation "..."`
- Complete: `desloppify plan triage --complete --strategy "..." --attestation "..."`

**Key capability**: Desloppify is designed to emit structured prompts and consume structured results from external agents. The skill document (`docs/SKILL.md`) has an explicit "hermes" overlay (v6, updated Feb 2025).

### 4. **Is it a Python package/module?**

**Yes, fully packaged:**

- **Entry point**: `desloppify = "desloppify.cli:main"` (pyproject.toml)
- **Language**: Python 3.11+
- **Distribution**: PyPI (`pip install desloppify[full]`)
- **Installation modes**: 
  - `pip install desloppify` (base, no optional deps)
  - `pip install desloppify[full]` (all: tree-sitter, Pillow for scorecards, bandit, PyYAML)
  - Language-specific subsets: `[treesitter]`, `[csharp-xml]`, `[python-security]`, `[scorecard]`, `[plan-yaml]`
- **Version**: 0.9.9 (as of recent commits)

### 5. **Does it already have IPC, HTTP, or communication mechanisms?**

**No native IPC/HTTP server.** Instead, it uses:

1. **File-based State & Persistence**:
   - `.desloppify/state-*.json` — issues, scores, scan metadata (per language)
   - `.desloppify/plan.json` — ordered queue, clusters, triage state
   - `.desloppify/query.json` — cached query for inspection
   - `.desloppify/config.json` — project config
   - Atomic writes with `.bak` recovery files

2. **Structured JSON I/O for Agent Integration**:
   - **Input packets** (for review): `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` (code samples + dimensions to score)
   - **Import contracts**: JSON files with dimensions, scores, findings → import with `--import FILE`
   - **External review sessions**: `.desloppify/external_review_sessions/` (per-session state)

3. **Query engine** (`base/search/`): Ripgrep-based file content search

4. **Lifecycle hooks** (`engine._plan.refresh_lifecycle`):
   - `transition_messages` in config — agent directives at phases (execute, postflight, triage, review, scan)
   - Phase-aware prompts from `plan triage --stage-prompt <stage>`

**No REST API, no socket server, no daemon.** It's a CLI tool designed to be invoked by external orchestrators (Hermes, Claude Code, etc.) that consume/produce JSON.

### 6. **What's its entry point / how is it invoked?**

**Entry point**: `desloppify.cli:main()`

**User-facing invocation**:
```bash
desloppify scan [--path .] [--skip-slow] [--lang python]
desloppify next [--count N]
desloppify review --prepare
desloppify review --import results.json --attest "reason"
desloppify plan triage --stage-prompt observe
desloppify resolve <pattern> --status fixed --note "reason"
desloppify show --query "tier:1"
desloppify status
```

**CLI Structure**:
- `desloppify` (top-level) → parser dispatches to 18+ subcommands
- Parser built in `app/cli_support/parser.py` using argparse groups
- Each command module in `app/commands/<name>/cmd.py` implements a `cmd_<name>` function
- Central registry in `app/commands/registry.py` maps command names to handlers

**State Loading**: Every command loads:
- `.desloppify/config.json` (project settings)
- `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json` (detected issues)
- `.desloppify/plan.json` (work queue)

### 7. **Any existing hooks or integration points?**

**Yes, several sophisticated hook systems:**

**a) Language Hooks** (`engine/hook_registry.py`):
   - `register_lang_hook(lang, hook_type, fn)` — allows languages to inject behavior
   - Types: complexity metrics, zone refinement, import resolution, AST walking
   - Detectors call `get_lang_hook(...)` to specialize behavior without language imports

**b) Plugin System** (`languages/_framework/`):
   - Auto-discovered language modules in `languages/<name>/`
   - Each language defines: phases (structural, style, security), detectors, fixers, review dimensions
   - Framework normalizes raw tool output (linters, tree-sitter) → tiered findings
   - 8 full plugins (Python, TypeScript, C#, C++, Dart, GDScript, Go, Rust)
   - 21 generic plugins (fallback to linters + tree-sitter)

**c) Detector Registry** (`base/registry.py`):
   - All 100+ detectors registered in `catalog_entries.py`
   - Runtime registration: `register_detector(meta)` with callbacks
   - Allows third-party detectors to be plugged in

**d) Agent Directives** (`app/commands/directives.py`):
   - `transition_messages` in config: text shown at lifecycle phase boundaries
   - Phases: execute, postflight, triage, review, scan
   - Agents can call `desloppify directives show` or `desloppify directives set <phase> "<message>"`

**e) Workflow Hints** (`app/commands/scan/reporting/agent_context.py`):
   - `auto_update_skill()` — checks if skill doc needs refresh
   - Detects installed skill overlays: Claude, Hermes, Cursor, Copilot, Windsurf, Gemini, AMP, OpenCode
   - Emits version warnings if skill doc is stale

**f) Subjective Review Integration** (`intelligence/review/`):
   - Batch packet generation: `prepare_batches_builders.py`, `prepare_holistic_batches.py`
   - Dimension selection: `selection.py` picks which subjective axes to score
   - Import contract: `importing/cmd.py` validates & merges external scoring results
   - Integrity checks: `assessment_integrity.py` ensures scores match target range

**g) Triage Orchestration** (`app/commands/plan/triage/`):
   - Stages: observe, reflect, organize, enrich, sense-check
   - Each stage has a prompt generator (`stage_prompts_*.py`)
   - Output validation (`stage_validation.py`)
   - Can run sequentially or in parallel with Hermes worktree sessions

**h) Config Hooks** (`base/config/`):
   - Schema-driven config (`config/schema.py`)
   - Migration layer for backward compatibility
   - Backup/recovery (`.json.bak` files for corruption recovery)

---

### **Summary Table**

| Aspect | Details |
|--------|---------|
| **Purpose** | Multi-language codebase health scanner with subjective LLM review |
| **Type** | Python 3.11+ CLI tool; pip-installable |
| **Commands** | 18+: scan, next, review, resolve, plan, show, status, backlog, exclude, directives, move, detect, zone, autofix, etc. |
| **State Location** | `.desloppify/` directory (config.json, state-*.json, plan.json, review_packets/) |
| **Languages** | 29 supported: 8 full plugins, 21 generic fallbacks |
| **Scoring** | Objective (mechanical) + subjective (LLM-reviewed). Weighted, anti-gaming. |
| **Hermes Integration** | Review workflow (blind packet → parallel sessions) + triage workflow (per-stage prompts + worktree) |
| **Communication** | File-based JSON (no HTTP/IPC server). Designed for external orchestration. |
| **Hooks** | Language hooks, detector registry, plugin system, agent directives, workflow hints, review imports |
| **Key Files** | `cli.py` (entry), `state_io.py` (persistence), `engine/` (core logic), `app/commands/` (CLI), `languages/` (plugins) |

**For Hermes integration specifically**: Desloppify provides structured prompts, consumes structured results, and tracks progress across sessions. The relationship is **orchestrator + tool**, not **daemon + client**. Hermes calls desloppify subcommands, reads/writes JSON files in `.desloppify/`, and coordinates parallel work via worktree sessions.

    62→def cmd_directives(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    63→    """Handle directives subcommands: show, set, unset."""
    64→    action = getattr(args, "directives_action", None)
    65→    if action == "set":
    66→        _directives_set(args)
    67→    elif action == "unset":
    68→        _directives_unset(args)
    69→    else:
    70→        _directives_show(args)
    71→
    72→
    73→def _directives_show(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    74→    """Show all phases with their directives (if configured)."""
    75→    config = command_runtime(args).config
    76→    messages = config.get("transition_messages", {})
    77→    if not isinstance(messages, dict):
    78→        messages = {}
    79→
    80→    active = {
    81→        phase: text
    82→        for phase, text in messages.items()
    83→        if isinstance(text, str) and text.strip()
    84→    }
    85→
    86→    print(colorize("\n  Agent Directives\n", "bold"))
    87→    print(
    88→        "  Messages shown to AI agents at lifecycle phase transitions.\n"
    89→        "  Use them to switch models, set constraints, or give context-\n"
    90→        "  specific instructions at key moments in the workflow.\n"
    91→    )
    92→
    93→    for name, description, when, example_use in _PHASES:

    95→        if directive:
    96→            marker = colorize("*", "green")
    97→            print(f"  {marker} {colorize(name, 'cyan')}  {description}")
    98→            print(colorize(f"    When: {when}", "dim"))
    99→            print(f"    Directive: {directive}")
   100→        else:
   101→            print(f"    {colorize(name, 'cyan')}  {description}")
   102→            print(colorize(f"    When: {when}", "dim"))
   103→            print(colorize(f"    e.g.: {example_use}", "dim"))
   104→        print()
   105→
   106→    count = len(active)
   107→    if count:
   108→        print(colorize(f"  {count} directive{'s' if count != 1 else ''} configured.\n", "green"))
   109→    else:
   110→        print(colorize("  No directives configured.\n", "dim"))
   111→
   112→    print(colorize("  Examples:", "dim"))
   113→    for phase, text in _EXAMPLE_DIRECTIVES.items():
   114→        print(colorize(f'    desloppify directives set {phase} "{text}"', "dim"))
   115→    print()

   118→    print(colorize("    desloppify directives unset <phase>", "dim"))
   119→    print()

   121→
   122→def _directives_set(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   123→    """Set a directive for a lifecycle phase."""
   124→    phase = args.phase
   125→    text = args.message
   126→
   127→    # Accept the main phases (including postflight) plus any valid lifecycle phase.
   128→    if phase != "postflight" and phase not in VALID_LIFECYCLE_PHASES:
   129→        valid = ", ".join(sorted(_PHASE_NAMES))
   130→        raise CommandError(f"unknown phase {phase!r}; valid phases: {valid}")
   131→
   132→    config = command_runtime(args).config
   133→    messages = config.get("transition_messages", {})
   134→    if not isinstance(messages, dict):
   135→        messages = {}
   136→    messages[phase] = text
   137→    config["transition_messages"] = messages
   138→
   139→    try:
   140→        save_config(config)
   141→    except OSError as e:
   142→        raise CommandError(f"could not save config: {e}") from e
   143→    print(colorize(f"  Set directive for {phase}:", "green"))
   144→    print(f"    {text}")
   145→
   146→
   147→def _directives_unset(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   148→    """Remove a directive for a lifecycle phase."""
   149→    phase = args.phase
   150→
   151→    config = command_runtime(args).config
   152→    messages = config.get("transition_messages", {})
   153→    if not isinstance(messages, dict):
   154→        messages = {}
   155→
   156→    if phase not in messages:
   157→        raise CommandError(f"no directive set for phase {phase!r}")
   158→
   159→    del messages[phase]
   160→    config["transition_messages"] = messages
   161→
   162→    try:
   163→        save_config(config)
   164→    except OSError as e:
   165→        raise CommandError(f"could not save config: {e}") from e
   166→    print(colorize(f"  Removed directive for {phase}", "green"))
   167→

1→"""Emit user-configured messages at lifecycle phase transitions."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.config import load_config
     8→from desloppify.base.output.user_message import print_user_message
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.refresh_lifecycle import (
    10→    COARSE_PHASE_MAP,
    11→    LIFECYCLE_PHASE_EXECUTE,
    12→    LIFECYCLE_PHASE_SCAN,
    13→)
    14→
    15→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    16→
    17→# Phases that are NOT postflight — everything else counts as postflight.
    18→_NON_POSTFLIGHT = frozenset({LIFECYCLE_PHASE_EXECUTE, LIFECYCLE_PHASE_SCAN})
    19→
    20→
    21→def emit_transition_message(new_phase: str) -> bool:
    22→    """Print a transition message if one is configured for *new_phase*.
    23→
    24→    Lookup order: exact phase → coarse phase → ``postflight`` (if the
    25→    phase is not execute/scan).
    26→
    27→    Returns True if a message was emitted.
    28→    """
    29→    try:
    30→        config = load_config()
    31→    except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
    32→        logger.debug("transition message skipped (config load): %s", exc)
    33→        return False
    34→
    35→    messages = config.get("transition_messages")
    36→    if not isinstance(messages, dict) or not messages:
    37→        return False
    38→
    39→    # Try exact phase first, then coarse fallback, then postflight.
    40→    text = messages.get(new_phase)
    41→    if text is None:
    42→        coarse = COARSE_PHASE_MAP.get(new_phase)
    43→        if coarse and coarse != new_phase:
    44→            text = messages.get(coarse)
    45→    if text is None and new_phase not in _NON_POSTFLIGHT:
    46→        text = messages.get("postflight")
    47→
    48→    if not isinstance(text, str) or not text.strip():
    49→        return False
    50→
    51→    clean = text.strip()
    52→    print(f"\n{'─' * 60}")
    53→    print(f"TRANSITION INSTRUCTION — entering {new_phase} phase")
    54→    print(clean)
    55→    print(f"{'─' * 60}")
    56→    print_user_message(f"Hey, did you see the above? Please act on this: {clean}")
    57→    return True

1→"""Shared triage lifecycle helpers used by command and runner entrypoints."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections.abc import Callable, Mapping

     9→from desloppify.engine.plan_state import PlanModel
    10→from desloppify.engine.plan_triage import (
    11→    TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS,
    12→    TriageStartDecision,
    13→    decide_triage_start,
    14→)

    18→)

    22→from .review_coverage import ensure_active_triage_issue_ids
    23→from .services import TriageServices
    24→from .stage_queue import has_triage_in_queue, inject_triage_stages
    25→
    26→TriageStartStatus = Literal["started", "blocked", "already_active"]
    27→
    28→
    29→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    30→class TriageStartOutcome:
    31→    """Lifecycle result for triage-start attempts."""
    32→
    33→    status: TriageStartStatus
    34→    reason: str
    35→
    36→
    37→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    38→class TriageLifecycleDeps:
    39→    """Dependency seams for triage lifecycle starts (for tests/callers)."""
    40→
    41→    has_triage_in_queue: Callable[[PlanModel], bool] = has_triage_in_queue
    42→    inject_triage_stages: Callable[[PlanModel], None] = inject_triage_stages
    43→    decide_triage_start: Callable[..., TriageStartDecision] = decide_triage_start
    44→    colorize: Callable[[str, str], str] = colorize
    45→
    46→
    47→def _print_triage_start_block(reason: str, *, deps: TriageLifecycleDeps) -> None:
    48→    """Render reason-specific guard guidance for triage auto-start attempts."""
    49→    if reason == "unfinished_triage_stage_records":
    50→        print(
    51→            deps.colorize(
    52→                "  Cannot start triage while previous stage reports are still unconfirmed.",
    53→                "red",
    54→            )
    55→        )
    56→        print(
    57→            deps.colorize(
    58→                "  Confirm or complete the current triage stages first, or pass --attestation "
    59→                "(30+ chars) to override explicitly.",
    60→                "dim",
    61→            )
    62→        )
    63→        return
    64→
    65→    print(deps.colorize("  Triage is pending behind the current objective backlog.", "red"))
    66→    print(
    67→        deps.colorize(
    68→            "  Finish current objective work first, or pass --attestation "
    69→            "(30+ chars) to override this guard explicitly.",
    70→            "dim",
    71→        )
    72→    )
    73→
    74→
    75→def ensure_triage_started(
    76→    plan: PlanModel,
    77→    *,
    78→    services: TriageServices,
    79→    state: StateModel | None = None,
    80→    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None = None,
    81→    attestation: str | None = None,
    82→    log_action: str | None = None,
    83→    log_actor: str = "system",
    84→    log_detail: Mapping[str, object] | None = None,
    85→    start_message: str | None = None,
    86→    start_message_style: str = "cyan",
    87→    deps: TriageLifecycleDeps | None = None,
    88→) -> TriageStartOutcome:
    89→    """Ensure triage stages are injected or return a blocked/already-active outcome."""
    90→    resolved_deps = deps or TriageLifecycleDeps()
    91→    meta = plan.setdefault("epic_triage_meta", {})
    92→    resolved_policy = policy
    93→    if resolved_policy is None and state is not None:
    94→        resolved_policy = compute_subjective_visibility(state, plan=plan)
    95→
    96→    if resolved_deps.has_triage_in_queue(plan):
    97→        if state is not None:
    98→            ensure_active_triage_issue_ids(plan, state)
    99→            services.save_plan(plan)
   100→        meta.pop("triage_start_blocked", None)
   101→        return TriageStartOutcome(status="already_active", reason="already_active")
   102→
   103→    decision = resolved_deps.decide_triage_start(
   104→        plan,
   105→        state,
   106→        policy=resolved_policy,
   107→        explicit_start=True,
   108→        attested_override=bool(attestation and len(attestation.strip()) >= 30),
   109→    )
   110→    if decision.action == "defer":
   111→        meta["triage_start_blocked"] = decision.reason
   112→        services.save_plan(plan)
   113→        _print_triage_start_block(decision.reason, deps=resolved_deps)
   114→        return TriageStartOutcome(status="blocked", reason=decision.reason)
   115→
   116→    resolved_deps.inject_triage_stages(plan)
   117→    meta.pop("triage_start_blocked", None)
   118→    meta.setdefault("triage_stages", {})
   119→    if state is not None:
   120→        ensure_active_triage_issue_ids(plan, state)
   121→
   122→    if log_action:
   123→        detail = dict(log_detail or {})
   124→        detail.setdefault("injected_stage_ids", list(TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS))
   125→        services.append_log_entry(
   126→            plan,
   127→            log_action,
   128→            actor=log_actor,
   129→            detail=detail,
   130→        )
   131→
   132→    services.save_plan(plan)
   133→    if start_message:
   134→        print(
   135→            resolved_deps.colorize(
   136→                start_message,
   137→                start_message_style,
   138→            )
   139→        )
   140→    return TriageStartOutcome(status="started", reason=decision.reason)
   141→
   142→
   143→__all__ = [
   144→    "TriageLifecycleDeps",
   145→    "TriageStartOutcome",
   146→    "ensure_triage_started",
   147→]
   148→

     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import Iterable
     6→
     7→from desloppify.engine._plan.constants import SYNTHETIC_PREFIXES
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import PlanModel, ensure_plan_defaults
     9→from desloppify.engine._state.issue_semantics import counts_toward_objective_backlog
    10→
    11→[REDACTED]
    12→[REDACTED]
    13→_LIFECYCLE_PHASE_KEY = "lifecycle_phase"
    14→
    15→LIFECYCLE_PHASE_REVIEW_INITIAL = "review_initial"
    16→LIFECYCLE_PHASE_ASSESSMENT_POSTFLIGHT = "assessment_postflight"
    17→LIFECYCLE_PHASE_REVIEW_POSTFLIGHT = "review_postflight"
    18→LIFECYCLE_PHASE_WORKFLOW_POSTFLIGHT = "workflow_postflight"
    19→LIFECYCLE_PHASE_TRIAGE_POSTFLIGHT = "triage_postflight"
    20→LIFECYCLE_PHASE_EXECUTE = "execute"
    21→LIFECYCLE_PHASE_SCAN = "scan"
    22→
    23→# Coarse lifecycle names remain valid persisted values for older plan data.
    24→LIFECYCLE_PHASE_REVIEW = "review"
    25→LIFECYCLE_PHASE_WORKFLOW = "workflow"
    26→LIFECYCLE_PHASE_TRIAGE = "triage"
    27→
    28→COARSE_PHASE_MAP = {
    29→    LIFECYCLE_PHASE_REVIEW_INITIAL: LIFECYCLE_PHASE_REVIEW,
    30→    LIFECYCLE_PHASE_ASSESSMENT_POSTFLIGHT: LIFECYCLE_PHASE_REVIEW,
    31→    LIFECYCLE_PHASE_REVIEW_POSTFLIGHT: LIFECYCLE_PHASE_REVIEW,
    32→    LIFECYCLE_PHASE_WORKFLOW_POSTFLIGHT: LIFECYCLE_PHASE_WORKFLOW,
    33→    LIFECYCLE_PHASE_TRIAGE_POSTFLIGHT: LIFECYCLE_PHASE_TRIAGE,
    34→    LIFECYCLE_PHASE_EXECUTE: LIFECYCLE_PHASE_EXECUTE,
    35→    LIFECYCLE_PHASE_SCAN: LIFECYCLE_PHASE_SCAN,
    36→    LIFECYCLE_PHASE_REVIEW: LIFECYCLE_PHASE_REVIEW,
    37→    LIFECYCLE_PHASE_WORKFLOW: LIFECYCLE_PHASE_WORKFLOW,
    38→    LIFECYCLE_PHASE_TRIAGE: LIFECYCLE_PHASE_TRIAGE,
    39→}
    40→
    41→VALID_LIFECYCLE_PHASES = frozenset(COARSE_PHASE_MAP)
    42→
    43→
    44→def _refresh_state(plan: PlanModel) -> dict[str, object]:
    45→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    46→    refresh_state = plan.get("refresh_state")
    47→    if not isinstance(refresh_state, dict):
    48→        refresh_state = {}
    49→        plan["refresh_state"] = refresh_state
    50→    return refresh_state
    51→
    52→
    53→def _is_real_queue_issue(issue_id: str) -> bool:
    54→    return not any(str(issue_id).startswith(prefix) for prefix in SYNTHETIC_PREFIXES)
    55→
    56→
    57→def _touches_objective_issue(
    58→    *,
    59→    issue_ids: Iterable[str] | None,
    60→    state: dict[str, object] | None,
    61→) -> bool:
    62→    if issue_ids is None:
    63→        return True
    64→
    65→    real_issue_ids = [
    66→        issue_id
    67→        for issue_id in issue_ids
    68→        if _is_real_queue_issue(str(issue_id))
    69→    ]
    70→    if not real_issue_ids:
    71→        return False
    72→    if not isinstance(state, dict):
    73→        return True
    74→
    75→    issues = state.get("work_items") or state.get("issues", {})
    76→    if not isinstance(issues, dict):
    77→        return True
    78→
    79→    objective_seen = False
    80→    for issue_id in real_issue_ids:
    81→        issue = issues.get(issue_id)
    82→        if not isinstance(issue, dict):
    83→            return True
    84→        if counts_toward_objective_backlog(issue):
    85→            objective_seen = True
    86→    return objective_seen
    87→
    88→
    89→def current_lifecycle_phase(plan: PlanModel) -> str | None:
    90→    """Return the persisted lifecycle phase, falling back for legacy plans."""
    91→    refresh_state = plan.get("refresh_state")
    92→    if isinstance(refresh_state, dict):
    93→        phase = refresh_state.get(_LIFECYCLE_PHASE_KEY)
    94→        if isinstance(phase, str) and phase in VALID_LIFECYCLE_PHASES:
    95→            return phase
    96→    if postflight_scan_pending(plan):
    97→        return LIFECYCLE_PHASE_SCAN
    98→    if plan.get("plan_start_scores"):
    99→        return LIFECYCLE_PHASE_EXECUTE
   100→    return None
   101→
   102→
   103→def set_lifecycle_phase(plan: PlanModel, phase: str) -> bool:
   104→    """Persist the current queue lifecycle phase."""
   105→    if phase not in VALID_LIFECYCLE_PHASES:
   106→        raise ValueError(f"Unsupported lifecycle phase: {phase}")
   107→    refresh_state = _refresh_state(plan)
   108→    if refresh_state.get(_LIFECYCLE_PHASE_KEY) == phase:
   109→        return False
   110→    refresh_state[_LIFECYCLE_PHASE_KEY] = phase
   111→    return True
   112→
   113→
   114→def coarse_lifecycle_phase(plan: PlanModel | None) -> str | None:
   115→    """Return the coarse lifecycle phase for persisted fine/coarse plan data."""
   116→    if not isinstance(plan, dict):
   117→        return None
   118→    phase = current_lifecycle_phase(plan)
   119→    if phase is None:
   120→        return None
   121→    return COARSE_PHASE_MAP.get(phase)
   122→
   123→
   124→def postflight_scan_pending(plan: PlanModel) -> bool:
   125→    """Return True when the current empty-queue boundary still needs a scan."""
   126→    refresh_state = plan.get("refresh_state")
   127→    if not isinstance(refresh_state, dict):
   128→        return True
   129→    return not isinstance(refresh_state.get(_POSTFLIGHT_SCAN_KEY), int)
   130→
   131→
   132→def mark_postflight_scan_completed(
   133→    plan: PlanModel,
   134→    *,
   135→    scan_count: int | None,
   136→) -> bool:
   137→    """Record that the scan stage completed for the current refresh cycle."""
   138→    refresh_state = _refresh_state(plan)
   139→    try:
   140→        normalized_scan_count = int(scan_count or 0)
   141→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   142→        normalized_scan_count = 0
   143→    if refresh_state.get(_POSTFLIGHT_SCAN_KEY) == normalized_scan_count:
   144→        return False
   145→    refresh_state[_POSTFLIGHT_SCAN_KEY] = normalized_scan_count
   146→    return True
   147→
   148→
   149→def subjective_review_completed_for_scan(
   150→    plan: PlanModel,
   151→    *,
   152→    scan_count: int | None,
   153→) -> bool:
   154→    """Return True when postflight subjective review finished for *scan_count*."""
   155→    refresh_state = plan.get("refresh_state")
   156→    if not isinstance(refresh_state, dict):
   157→        return False
   158→    try:
   159→        normalized_scan_count = int(scan_count or 0)
   160→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   161→        normalized_scan_count = 0
   162→    return refresh_state.get(_SUBJECTIVE_REVIEW_KEY) == normalized_scan_count
   163→
   164→
   165→def mark_subjective_review_completed(
   166→    plan: PlanModel,
   167→    *,
   168→    scan_count: int | None,
   169→) -> bool:
   170→    """Record that subjective review completed for the current postflight scan."""
   171→    refresh_state = _refresh_state(plan)
   172→    try:
   173→        normalized_scan_count = int(scan_count or 0)
   174→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   175→        normalized_scan_count = 0
   176→    if refresh_state.get(_SUBJECTIVE_REVIEW_KEY) == normalized_scan_count:
   177→        return False
   178→    refresh_state[_SUBJECTIVE_REVIEW_KEY] = normalized_scan_count
   179→    return True
   180→
   181→
   182→def clear_postflight_scan_completion(
   183→    plan: PlanModel,
   184→    *,
   185→    issue_ids: Iterable[str] | None = None,
   186→    state: dict[str, object] | None = None,
   187→) -> bool:
   188→    """Require a fresh scan after queue-changing work on objective issues."""
   189→    if not _touches_objective_issue(issue_ids=issue_ids, state=state):
   190→        return False
   191→    refresh_state = _refresh_state(plan)
   192→    if _POSTFLIGHT_SCAN_KEY not in refresh_state:
   193→        return False
   194→    refresh_state[_LIFECYCLE_PHASE_KEY] = LIFECYCLE_PHASE_EXECUTE
   195→    refresh_state.pop(_POSTFLIGHT_SCAN_KEY, None)
   196→    return True
   197→
